as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
is finite, as asserted in (
4.26
), we introduce two decompositions to work with. The first one is similar to those in (
4.28
) and (
4.42
). We write

\displaystyle I^{2,4}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)=I^{2,4,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)+I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T),

(4.50)

where

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s-r^{\prime}){g}_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s-r^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}y+x,{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})-P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})]P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},

\displaystyle I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon},\infty)}(s-r^{\prime}){g}_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s-r^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}y+x,{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})-P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})]P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

The second decomposition we need is stated as follows:

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)=I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)+I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T),

(4.51)

where

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s^{\prime}}(y+\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})-P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})]P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times[{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)-{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)]

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s^{\prime}}(y+\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})-P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})]P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

To see (
4.51
), we apply the substitions
s^{\prime}={\varepsilon}^{-2}(s-r^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{x}={\varepsilon}^{-1}(x-y^{\prime})
for
s
and
x
to the definition of
I^{2,4,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
. This gives

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle=\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime}){g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}y+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime})-P_{{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime})]P_{{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}y{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\varepsilon}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime}){g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s^{\prime}}(y+\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})-P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})]P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},

where the last equality is obtained by making some cancellations of
{\varepsilon}
on its left-hand side. The decomposition in (
4.51
) now follows at once by adding the integrands of
I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
and
I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
.

In the following sub-steps, we prove, in the same order, that the limit superiors of the two terms
{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]
and
{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]
as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
are both finite, whereas the limit
{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]
is zero. A subsequent sub-step concludes Step 4.

Step 4-1.
To prove the required property of
I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
, first, we integrate out
\widetilde{x}
in its integral representation and use the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation. This gives the following expression:

\displaystyle I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)

\displaystyle=\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)[P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)-P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

Since
\phi(\cdot)
has a compact support, it follows from Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T)\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\lvert{g}_{\varepsilon}\rvert(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)\lvert P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)-P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

To improve the bound in the preceding inequality, observe that

\displaystyle\forall\;\lvert y\rvert\leq M,\quad

\displaystyle\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\lvert P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)-P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})\rvert\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathds{1}_{\{s^{\prime}\leq 1\}}C(\phi)+\mathds{1}_{\{s^{\prime}>1\}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\left|P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)-\frac{1}{4\pi s^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4\pi s^{\prime}}-P_{2s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})\right|\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathds{1}_{\{s^{\prime}\leq 1\}}C(\phi)+\mathds{1}_{\{s^{\prime}>1\}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\frac{1}{4\pi s^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert^{2}}{4s^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4\pi s^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert^{2}}{4s^{\prime}}\biggr)\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\phi,M)}{(s^{\prime})^{2}\vee 1},

where the second inequality follows since
0\leq 1-{\mathrm{e}}^{-x}\leq x
for all
x\geq 0
, and the last inequality follows since
\phi
has a compact support and we assume
\lvert y\rvert\leq M
. Since

{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)=0
for all
\lvert y\rvert>M
by the assumed bound, the foregoing bound applies, and we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,M)\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{y}\frac{\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})}{(s^{\prime})^{2}\vee 1}\lvert{g}_{\varepsilon}\rvert(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,M)\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{s=0}^{T-r^{\prime}}\int_{y}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)^{2}\vee 1}\lvert{g}_{\varepsilon}\rvert(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+s,T){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s\\
&\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},\end{split}

(4.52)

where the last inequality uses the substitution
s={\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}
.

To proceed, we use the finer details of the assumed bound on
{g}_{\varepsilon}
, with
m\geq 10
, to obtain a further bound on
{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]
. Hence, by (
4.52
),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{s=0}^{T-r^{\prime}}\int_{y}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)^{2}\vee 1}\times\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{s=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)^{2}\vee 1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime},

(4.53)

where the last inequality follows by applying (
4.7
) and substituting
r^{\prime\prime}=T-r^{\prime}
, then integrating out
y
and
y^{\prime}
.

Finally, we use (
4.53
) by writing
\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)=\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon}^{2},{\varepsilon})}(s)+\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{2})}(s)
, noting that
{\varepsilon}^{-2}s\geq 1
if and only if
s\geq{\varepsilon}^{2}
, and interchanging the order of integration from
{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}
to
{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s
. Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{s=0}^{T}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon}^{2},{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T}\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{s=0}^{T}{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{2})}(s)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T}\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s.

To bound the limit superior of the right-hand side, it suffices to observe that

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon}^{2},{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{s=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}T}\frac{\mathds{1}_{[1,{\varepsilon}^{-1})(s)}}{s^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}s,

(4.54)

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}{\varepsilon}^{-2}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{2})}(s){\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{s=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}T}\mathds{1}_{[0,1)}(s){\mathrm{d}}s,

(4.55)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T}\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{s+\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4}}\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}=\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4}}\lvert\log(4r^{\prime\prime}{\varepsilon}^{-2})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}=\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4}\int_{r^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{1}\lvert\log r^{\prime\prime\prime}\rvert{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime\prime},

(4.56)

where the last equality uses the substitution
r^{\prime\prime\prime}=4r^{\prime\prime}{\varepsilon}^{-2}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\lim\sup_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,1}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]<\infty,

(4.57)

which achieves the goal of this sub-step.

Step 4-2.
Recall that
I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
is defined below (
4.51
). To prove that the
L^{1}({\mathbb{P}})
-norm of
I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
remains bounded as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
, we start by showing an estimate for the
{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}
-integral in
I^{2,4,1,2}_{\varepsilon}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
. Recall that, by assumption,
x\mapsto g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Hence, by the mean value theorem and the assumed bound on
\nabla{g}_{\varepsilon}
, we get

\displaystyle\lvert{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)-{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert\sup_{x:\lvert x-y^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert}\lvert\nabla_{x}{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,x,r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim C(G,m)\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert\sup_{x:\lvert x-y^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim C(G,m)(\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert+\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert^{m+1})\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right],

(4.58)

where the last inequality holds since, whenever
\lvert x-y^{\prime}\rvert\leq\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert
, by (
7.8
),

1/(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})\leq C(m)(1+\lvert x-y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})/(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})\leq C(m)(1+\lvert{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x}\rvert^{m})/(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}).

Additionally, for any integer
k\geq 1
and any
\hat{y}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{x}}\lvert\widetilde{x}\rvert^{k}P_{s^{\prime}}(\hat{y},\widetilde{x})P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}=(\sqrt{2s^{\prime}})^{k}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\left|\frac{\widetilde{x}}{\sqrt{2s^{\prime}}}\right|^{k}P_{s^{\prime}}(\hat{y},\widetilde{x})P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(k)(\sqrt{2s^{\prime}})^{k}\int_{\widetilde{x}}P_{s^{\prime}}(\hat{y},\widetilde{x})P_{2s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}=C(k)(\sqrt{s^{\prime}})^{k}P_{3s^{\prime}}(\hat{y}),

(4.59)

where the inequality follows from the fact that
x^{k}e^{-x}\leq C(k){\mathrm{e}}^{-x/2}
for all
x\geq 0
, and the final equality is a consequence of the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation.
By (
4.58
) and (
4.59
), the
{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}
-integral in
I^{2,4,1,2}_{\varepsilon}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{\widetilde{x}}[{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}\widetilde{x},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)-{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime},T)]

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{s^{\prime}}(y+\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})-P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x},y^{\prime\prime})]P_{s^{\prime}}(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C({G},m)(\lvert{\varepsilon}\sqrt{s^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert{\varepsilon}\sqrt{s^{\prime}}\rvert^{m+1})\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times[P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)+P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})].

We proceed to the first bound of
{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]
.
By the last inequality and Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,{G},m,M)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T-r^{\prime})}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})(\lvert{\varepsilon}\sqrt{s^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert{\varepsilon}\sqrt{s^{\prime}}\rvert^{m+1})\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)+P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

(4.60)

Here, the integral with respect to
{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}
satisfies the following bound:

\displaystyle\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}\left[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times[P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y)+P_{3s^{\prime}}(y^{\prime\prime})]\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\phi,m,M)}{(s^{\prime})\vee 1}\biggl[\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(T-r^{\prime}-{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr],

where the last inequality follows by integrating out
y^{\prime\prime}
,
y^{\prime}
and
y
in the same order and using the following bound when integrating out
y^{\prime\prime}
:

\int_{y}P_{3s^{\prime}}(\hat{y},y)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\leq\frac{C(\phi)}{(s^{\prime})\vee 1},\quad\forall\;\hat{y}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

as well as (
4.7
) when integrating out
y
. By using the bound just obtained for the integral with respect to
{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}
, as well as
the substitutions
r^{\prime\prime}=T-r^{\prime}
and
s={\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}
, (
4.60
) implies the first inequality below:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,{G},m,M)\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{s=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)\frac{(\sqrt{s}+\sqrt{s}^{m+1})}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)\vee 1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\varepsilon}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}

(4.61)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,{G},m,M)\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{s=0}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sqrt{s}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon})}(s)}{({\varepsilon}^{-2}s)\vee 1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}.

(4.62)

Observe that the right-hand side is similar to the right-hand side of (
4.53
), though not the same. Hence, we rewrite the foregoing inequality as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{s=0}^{T}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sqrt{s}\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon}^{2},{\varepsilon})}(s)}{{\varepsilon}^{-2}s}

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T}\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad+C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,G,m,M)\int_{s=0}^{T}{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sqrt{s}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{2})}(s)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T}\biggl[\lvert\log(r^{\prime\prime}-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(r^{\prime\prime}-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s.

To show that the right-hand side remains bounded as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
, we apply (
4.56
) once more, but this time we replace (
4.54
) and (
4.55
) with the following estimates, using the substitution
s^{\prime}={\varepsilon}^{-2}s
:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\frac{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sqrt{s}\mathds{1}_{[{\varepsilon}^{2},{\varepsilon})}(s)}{{\varepsilon}^{-2}s}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}T}\frac{\sqrt{{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{[1,{\varepsilon}^{-1})}(s^{\prime})}{s^{\prime}}{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}\leq{\varepsilon}\cdot 2[\sqrt{{\varepsilon}^{-2}(T\vee 1)}-1],

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}{\varepsilon}^{-2}\sqrt{s}\mathds{1}_{[0,{\varepsilon}^{2})}(s){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle=\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}T}\sqrt{{\varepsilon}^{2}s^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{[0,1)}(s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}.

Hence, we have the following analogue of (
4.57
):

\displaystyle\limsup_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,1,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]<\infty.

(4.63)

Step 4-3.
Lastly, we show that the
L^{1}({\mathbb{P}})
-norm of
I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
tends to zero, where
I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
is defined below (
4.50
). Since
{g}_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)=0
whenever
\lvert y\rvert>M
by assumption, (
7.3
) with
\delta={\varepsilon}
gives the following bound:

\displaystyle\lvert I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}\lvert{g}_{\varepsilon}\rvert(y,x,s,T)

\displaystyle\quad\times C(M){\varepsilon}^{1/2}P_{2(s-r^{\prime})}(x,{\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(G,m,M,T){\varepsilon}^{1/2}\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{y}\mathds{1}_{\{\lvert y\rvert\leq M\}}\frac{P_{4(s-r^{\prime})}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime})}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[\lvert\log(T-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y\rvert\rvert+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},

(4.64)

where the last inequality follows from applying the assumed bound on
{g}_{\varepsilon}
with
m\geq 10
, together with (
7.11
) when integrating out
x
. By integrating out
y
and using (
4.7
), (
4.64
) implies

\displaystyle\lvert I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C({G},m,M,T){\varepsilon}^{1/2}\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\frac{P_{4(s-r^{\prime})}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime})}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(T-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\times\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}.

By Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
), the foregoing bound implies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,{G},m,M){\varepsilon}^{1/2}\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\frac{P_{4(s-r^{\prime})}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime})}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(T-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}s\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

(4.65)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,{G},m,M){\varepsilon}^{1/2}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{s}P_{4(s-r^{\prime})}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl[\lvert\log(T-s)\rvert+1+\lvert\log[4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}]\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4(T-s){\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}\biggr]\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s,

where the right-hand side follows by integrating out
y^{\prime}
and exchanging the order of integration so that
{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}={\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s
. By using Lemma
7.1
(1
∘
), the right-hand side of the last inequality tends to zero as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
. This proves

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4,2}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]=0.

(4.66)

Step 4-4.
Recall (
4.50
) and (
4.51
). Hence, by (
4.57
), (
4.63
), (
4.66
), we have proved that

\limsup_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert I^{2,4}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)\rvert]<\infty.

All required properties of
I^{2,j}_{{\varepsilon}}({g}_{\varepsilon},T)
for
j=1,2,3,4
, as stated in (
4.26
), have now been proved. This completes the proof of (1
∘
).

(2
∘
) By the definition (
2.40
) of
{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}
,
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\geq\pi/\log{\varepsilon}^{-1}
for all
{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]
. In this context, we can bound part of the integrand in (
4.27
) as follows by using (
4.4
): for
\varsigma\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\lvert R_{\ref{key}}(y^{\prime\prime}-\varsigma y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\lambda,T,f,m,M)}{(\log{\varepsilon}^{-1})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})}\left(\lvert\log(T-r^{\prime})\rvert+1+\lvert\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}-\varsigma y\rvert\rvert\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C(\lambda,T,f,m,M)}{(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})}{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y^{\prime\prime}-\varsigma y\rvert^{2}}\right).

Additionally, since
\phi
has a compact support,
we can use Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) to bound the expectation
{\mathbb{E}}[\Lambda_{{\varepsilon}}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{\varepsilon}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})]
, present in (
4.27
), simply by a constant
C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T)
. Hence, (
4.27
)
follows as soon as we show the following limits for all
m\geq 10
and
\varsigma\in\{0,1\}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\to 0}\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\frac{1}{(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})}\left|{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4(T-r^{\prime}){\varepsilon}^{-2}}{\lvert y^{\prime\prime}-\varsigma y\rvert^{2}}\right)\right|\phi(y)\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}=0.

To observe these limits, recall once again that
\phi
has compact support. Therefore, by (
4.7
), the two limits discussed above can be justified directly using the following formulas:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T}\min\left\{\frac{1}{4r^{\prime\prime}{\varepsilon}^{-2}},1\right\}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}&=\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}[\log T-\log(T\wedge\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4})]}{4}+T\wedge\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4},\\
\int_{r^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T}\lvert\log(4r^{\prime\prime}{\varepsilon}^{-2})\rvert\mathds{1}_{\{4r^{\prime\prime}{\varepsilon}^{-2}<1\}}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime}&={\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{0}^{({\varepsilon}^{-2}T)\wedge\frac{1}{4}}\lvert\log(4r^{\prime\prime\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime\prime\prime}.\end{aligned}

(4.67)

We have proved the required limit in (
4.27
). The proof of Lemma
4.5
is complete.

\blacksquare

End of the proof of Proposition
4.3
.

First, to prove (
4.19
) and (
4.20
), it suffices to show the following particular case, using test functions
g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{1}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}g(x,x)\psi(x)\nu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s),

(4.68)

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{y}g(y,x)\psi(y)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right)\nu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s).

(4.69)

First, to obtain (
4.68
), we consider the following equalities, where the limits use convergence in probability:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mu^{\sharp}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

(4.70)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x)\psi(x)\phi(y)\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,s)X_{n}(x,s){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x)\psi(x)\phi(y)\Lambda_{n}X_{n}(x,s)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

(4.71)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{y}g({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right)\nu^{\sharp}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}g(x,x)\psi(x)\nu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s).

(4.72)

The justifications for (
4.70
), (
4.71
), and (
4.72
) are as follows. Equation (
4.70
) follows from the almost-sure convergence of
\mu^{\sharp}_{n}
to
\mu^{\sharp}_{\infty}
in
C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})}[0,\infty)
(see Corollary
3.6
). Equation (
4.71
) is based on Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.5
(1
∘
), using the following choice of
(g,g_{\varepsilon})
:

g(y,x,s,T)=g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)\equiv g({\varepsilon}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}y+x)\psi(x)\phi(y).

Finally, (
4.72
) is justified by the uniform bound
\sup_{n}{\mathbb{E}}[\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}(1+\lvert x\rvert^{10})^{-1}\nu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)]<\infty
(from Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
)) and the almost-sure convergence of
\nu^{\sharp}_{n}
to
\nu^{\sharp}_{\infty}
in
C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)
. This completes the proof of (
4.68
).
The proof of (
4.69
) is almost the same:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}g(\widetilde{x},x)\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

(4.73)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g(y,x)\psi(y)\psi(x)\phi(y)\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,s)X_{n}(x,s){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g(y,x)\psi(y)\psi(x)\phi(y)\Lambda_{n}X_{n}(x,s)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

(4.74)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{y}g(y,x)\psi(y)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right)\nu^{\sharp}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{y}g(y,x)\psi(y)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right)\nu^{\sharp}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s).

(4.75)

In more detail, (
4.73
) holds by using the almost-sure convergence of
\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{n}
to
\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\infty}
in
C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})}[0,\infty)
. Equation (

g(y,x,s,T)=g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)\equiv g(y,x)\psi(y)\psi(x)\phi(y).

By (

), we have verified both (
4.68
) and (
4.69
).

The remainder of this proof is to establish the required limit of
I^{2}_{n}(f,T)
for
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{1}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, where

has been defined in the statement of Proposition

g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)\equiv\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon}^{1}f(y,x,s,T)
satisfies the assumption in Lemma

(1
∘
):

•

The

regularity in the variable

follows from Lemma

(2
∘
) and the dominated convergence theorem.

•

To see that
\lvert g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)\rvert=\lvert\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon}^{1}f(y,x,s,T)\rvert
can be bounded as in Lemma
4.5
(1
∘

holds.

•

The required bound on

follows directly from (
4.6
) and the compact support of
\phi
.

Given that
I^{2}_{n}(f,T)
admits the decomposition in (
4.22
) and Lemma
4.5
(1
∘
) applies to our choice of
g_{\varepsilon}(y,x,s,T)
, we obtain the first equality below, assuming the existence of the limit:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}I^{2}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-I^{2,4}_{n}(\mathfrak{L}^{1}_{n}f,T)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-I^{2,4}_{n}\big((y,x,s,T)\mapsto f(x,s)\phi(y),T\big),

(4.76)

where the second equality uses (
4.5
) and again Lemma

(1
∘
).

The next step is to verify the existence of the limit in probability of
-I^{2,4}_{n}\big((y,x,s,T)\mapsto f(x,s)\phi(y),T\big)
and to compute its value. For this purpose, we examine alternative limits in probability, as expressed by the following equations:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-I^{2,4}_{n}\big((y,x,s,T)\mapsto f(x,s)\phi(y),T\big)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}f(x,s)\phi(y)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times[P_{s-r^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}_{n}y+x,{\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})-P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,{\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime})]P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{n}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\int_{y}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,s)P_{s-r^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}-{\varepsilon}_{n}y+y^{\prime},x)P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{y}\int_{s=r^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,s)P_{s-r^{\prime}}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},x)P_{s-r^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{n}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\biggl(\Lambda_{n}^{-1}\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{n}f(y^{\prime\prime}-y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)-\Lambda_{n}^{-1}\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{n}f(y^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)\biggr)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r^{\prime})X_{n}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime},

(4.77)

where the first equality applies directly the formula of
I^{2,4}_{\varepsilon}(g,T)
, as defined in
Lemma
4.4
, and the last equality follows by using the definition (

) of
\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{n}f=\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}f
. To continue, we use (
4.2
) to get the first equality below:

\displaystyle\int_{y}\biggl(\Lambda_{n}^{-1}\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{n}f(y^{\prime\prime}-y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)-\Lambda_{n}^{-1}\mathring{\mathfrak{L}}^{1}_{n}f(y^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)\biggr)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad=\left[-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y}(\log\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y}(\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right]f(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left(\frac{\Lambda_{n}^{-1}}{\log{\varepsilon}_{n}^{-1}}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\right)\left[-\int_{y}(\log\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y+\int_{y}(\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right]f(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{y}\biggl(\Lambda_{n}^{-1}R_{\ref{key}}(y^{\prime\prime}-y,y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)-\Lambda_{n}^{-1}R_{\ref{key}}(y^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime},r^{\prime},T)\biggr)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y,

where
R_{\ref{key}}(y,x,s,T)
satisfies (
4.4
). By Lemma
4.5
(2
∘
) and Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
), applying the last equality to the right-hand side of (
4.77
) gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-I^{2,4}_{n}\big((y,x,s,T)\mapsto f(x,s)\phi(y),T\big)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{P}}\mbox{-}\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\left[-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y}(\log\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y}(\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y\right]f(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}y^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime},r)X_{n}(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\phi(y^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})(\log\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}\right)f(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{r^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})(\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}\right)f(y^{\prime},r^{\prime})\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}r^{\prime})

(4.78)

where the last inequality follows by using (
4.20
). We have proved
the existence of the limit in probability of
-I^{2,4}_{n}\big((y,x,s,T)\mapsto f(x,s)\phi(y),T\big)
and found its value.

Finally, combining (
4.76
) and (
4.78
) proves the required limit of
I^{2}_{n}(f,T)
. The proof of Proposition
4.3
is complete.

\blacksquare

4.3
Limit theorem for
\boldsymbol{I}^{\boldsymbol{3}}_{\boldsymbol{n}}\boldsymbol{(}\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{,}\boldsymbol{T})
and
\boldsymbol{I}^{\boldsymbol{3}}_{\boldsymbol{n}}\boldsymbol{(}\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{,}\boldsymbol{T}\boldsymbol{)}+\boldsymbol{I}^{\boldsymbol{4}}_{\boldsymbol{n}}\boldsymbol{(}\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{,}\boldsymbol{T}\boldsymbol{)}

We now consider the limiting formulas for the stochastic integral terms
I^{3}_{n}(f,T)
and
I^{3}_{n}(f,T)+I^{4}_{n}(f,T)
as
n\to\infty
. The main result is stated in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.6.

Let
T\in(0,\infty)
and
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T])
. Recall that
\mathfrak{L}^{3}_{0}f
and
\mathfrak{L}^{4}_{0}f
are defined as in (
3.19
) and (
3.20
). For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define the following stochastic integrals:

\displaystyle I^{3}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s),

(4.79)

\displaystyle I^{4}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{s=0}^{s^{\prime}}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{4}f(y^{\prime},y,s^{\prime},s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}).

(4.80)

Then
I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,I^{3}_{n}(f,T)+I^{4}_{n}(f,T)
satisfies the following formula:

\displaystyle I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle=\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}),\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{N},

(4.81)

and the following limits in probability hold:

\displaystyle I^{3}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{{\mathbb{P}}}I^{3}_{\infty}(f,T)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s),

(4.82)

\displaystyle I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{{\mathbb{P}}}I^{3,4}_{\infty}(f,T)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}).

(4.83)

Here, the stochastic integrals in (
4.79
)–(
4.83
) are well-defined, and the following bounds hold:

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{E}}[I^{3}_{n}(f,T)^{2}]<\infty,

(4.84)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{E}}[I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)^{2}]<\infty.

(4.85)

The proof of Proposition
4.6
relies on Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
), Lemma
4.7
, and Lemma
4.8
. The main goal of Lemma
4.7
is to specify a class of functions integrable against the martingale measure
M_{\infty}
and establish the limit behavior of
M_{n}
as
n\to\infty
. Although
M_{\infty}
is
defined via (
3.39
) using only
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
-test functions, it uniquely determines a measure
\langle M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
on
\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\otimes\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})
such that the first and second processes in

\displaystyle\{M_{\infty}(f,t)\}_{t\geq 0},\quad\left\{M_{\infty}(f,t)^{2}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right\}_{t\geq 0}

(4.86)

are
L^{4}
- and
L^{2}
-martingales, respectively, for any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Part (1
∘
) of Lemma
4.7
establishes the existence of
{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
, while introducing an identity that links this measure to
\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
, and subsequently to
\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
and
\mathring{\mu}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
via (
4.19
) and (
4.20
). Part (2
∘
)
uses
{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
to define an auxiliary norm, thereby enabling the standard
L^{2}
-construction of stochastic integrals against
M_{\infty}
under conditions analogous to those in (
4.84
) and (
4.85
) for
n=\infty
. For our purposes, part (2
∘
) restricts focus to
{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
-test functions, for which the
L^{4}
- and
L^{2}
-martingale properties of the processes in (
4.86
) remain valid. Finally, parts (3
∘
) and (4
∘
) address the limiting behavior of the projected martingale measures
M_{n}
as
n\to\infty
under different test function regularities, yielding distinct modes of convergence that apply directly to our proof of Proposition
4.6
.

Lemma 4.7.

(1
∘
)
The martingale measure
M_{\infty}
is orthogonal, meaning that its covariation measure
{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
is supported on
\{(x^{\prime},x)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2};x^{\prime}=x\}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
. Accordingly, it is written as
{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
henceforth. Additionally, for all
T\in(0,\infty)
and nonnegative
g\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
,

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}g(x,x,s){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}g(x^{\prime},x,s)\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s).

(4.87)

(2
∘
)
Let
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
.
Whenever
f_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, approximate
f
in the following norm:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{x}[f_{n}(x)-f(x)]^{2}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right]=0,\quad\forall\;T\in(0,\infty),

(4.88)

the sequence
\{M_{\infty}(f_{n},t);t\geq 0\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges uniformly on compacts in probability as
n\to\infty
. The limiting process does not depend on the choice of
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and defines a martingale denoted by
\{M_{\infty}(f,t);t\geq 0\}
. Moreover, the corresponding processes defined in (
4.86
) are
L^{4}
- and
L^{2}
-martingales.

(3
∘
)
With probability one, the following limit holds for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
:

\displaystyle\{M_{n}(f,t)\}_{t\geq 0}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\{M_{\infty}(f,t)\}_{t\geq 0}\mbox{ in }C_{{\mathbb{R}}}[0,\infty),

(4.89)

where
C_{{\mathbb{R}}}[0,\infty)
is equipped with the topology of uniform convergence on compacts.

(4
∘
)
For any
T\in(0,\infty)
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, the following limit in probability holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert M_{n}(f,t)-M_{\infty}(f,t)\rvert\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{{\mathbb{P}}}0.

(4.90)

Proof.
(1
∘
) The aim of this proof is to relate the covariation measure of
M_{\infty}
to
\mu_{\infty}
. For this purpose, observe that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
T\in(0,\infty)
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,
the two processes below are martingales:

\displaystyle\{M_{n}(f,t)\}_{0\leq t\leq T},\quad\left\{M_{n}(f,t)^{2}-\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime})f(x)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right\}_{0\leq t\leq T}.

(4.91)

This is a direct consequence of the definitions of
M_{n}
and
\mu_{n}
for finite
n
. Additionally, the martingales in (
4.91
) for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
are uniformly integrable since

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}[M_{n}(f,t)^{4}]<\infty,

(4.92)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(M_{n}(f,t)^{2}-\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime})f(x)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right)^{2}\right]<\infty.

(4.93)

Both bounds hold since by the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
61
, (4.1) Theorem, p.160]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}[M_{n}(f,t)^{4}]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}[\langle M_{n}(f,\cdot),M_{n}(f,\cdot){\rangle}_{T}^{2}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime})f(x)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)\lVert f\rVert_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}^{2},

(4.94)

where the last inequality follows from (
3.35c
) and Theorem
3.4
(1
∘
).

We now prove the required properties. By (
4.92
) and (
4.93
), a standard result of uniform integrability implies that the martingale property of the two processes in (
4.91
) extends to
n=\infty
. Therefore,
\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime})f(x)\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
, for
0\leq t\leq T
, must coincide with the quadratic variation of
M_{\infty}(f,t)
. Consequently, we deduce that the measure
\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)
, which is diagonal by (
3.35c
), represents the covariation measure of
M_{\infty}
via (
4.87
). This is enough to establish all the required properties in (1
∘
).

(2
∘
) All required properties, except the
L^{4}
- and
L^{2}
-boundeness of
the corresponding processes defined in (
4.86
), follow directly from Doob’s weak
L^{2}
-inequality
[
61
, (1.7) Theorem, p.54]
. The
L^{4}
- and
L^{2}
-boundedness can be deduced from (
4.94
).

(3
∘
) By (
3.39
), the required property follows directly from Corollary
3.8
.

(4
∘
) Any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
vanishes at infinity. Therefore, by mollification, there exists a sequence
\{f_{k}\}\subset{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
such that
f_{k}\to f
uniformly on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, and
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\lvert f_{k}(x)\rvert\leq C(f,m)(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})^{-1}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. (This bound is justified directly by (
7.8
).) Moreover, for any
T,\eta,{\varepsilon}>0
, there exists an integer
k_{0}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(M_{n}(f,t)-M_{n}(f_{k_{0}},t)\Big)^{2}\biggr]<{\varepsilon}\eta^{2}.

(4.95)

This follows by applying Doob’s strong
L^{2}
-inequality, in combination with (
3.3b
) and Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and with an extension to (
4.94
) to
n=\infty
for
n=\infty
. For the same
T,\eta,{\varepsilon}>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert M_{n}(f,t)-M_{\infty}(f,t)\rvert>3\eta\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(M_{n}(f,t)-M_{n}(f_{k_{0}},t)\Big)^{2}\biggr]+{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert M_{n}(f_{k_{0}},t)-M_{\infty}(f_{k_{0}},t)\rvert>\eta\biggr)+

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big(M_{\infty}(f_{k_{0}},t)-M_{\infty}(f,t)\Big)^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2{\varepsilon}+{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert M_{n}(f_{k_{0}},t)-M_{\infty}(f_{k_{0}},t)\rvert>\eta\biggr)\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}2{\varepsilon},

where the first inequality uses Markov’s inequality, the second inequality uses (
4.95
),
and the limit follows from (3
∘
) since
f_{k_{0}}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Since
{\varepsilon}>0
is arbitrary, the foregoing limit is enough to get (
4.90
).
The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

The next lemma collects key results on the uniform approximation of càdlàg functions with step functions, as established in
[
49
, pp.1066–1067]
. The general framework and main properties can be restated as follows. Let
(E,r)
be a metric space, and consider a sequence of i.i.d. random variables
\{U_{k}(\omega^{\prime})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
defined on a probability space
(\Omega^{\prime},\mathscr{F}^{\prime},{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime})
, each uniformly distributed over the interval
[\frac{1}{2},1]
. For convenience, assume that
U_{k}(\omega^{\prime})
takes values in the same interval for all
\omega^{\prime}
. Given any
\theta>0
and
\mathfrak{z}\in D_{E}[0,\infty)
, inductively define the following sequence of times for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
:

\displaystyle\tau^{\theta}_{0}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{0}(\mathfrak{z})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,0,

(4.96a)

\displaystyle\tau^{\theta}_{k+1}(\mathfrak{z})

\displaystyle=\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k+1}(\mathfrak{z})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\inf\left\{t>\tau^{\theta}_{k}(\mathfrak{z});r\big(\mathfrak{z}(t),\mathfrak{z}(\tau^{\theta}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}))\big)\vee r\big(\mathfrak{z}(t-),\mathfrak{z}(\tau^{\theta}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}))\big)\geq\theta U_{k}\right\}.

(4.96b)

The associated step function that approximates
\mathfrak{z}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle I^{\theta}(\mathfrak{z})(t)=I^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{z})(t)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{z}(\tau^{\theta}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}))\mathds{1}_{[\tau^{\theta}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}),\tau^{\theta}_{k+1}(\mathfrak{z}))}(t).

(4.97)

Lemma 4.8 (Kurtz and Protter
[
49
]
).

Fix
\theta>0
.

(1
∘
)
For all
\mathfrak{z}\in D_{E}[0,\infty)
,
\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k}(\mathfrak{z})\nearrow\infty
as
k\to\infty
for all
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
.

(2
∘
)
For all
\mathfrak{z}\in D_{E}[0,\infty)
,

r\big(\mathfrak{z}(t),I^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{z})(t)\big)\leq\theta
for all
t\geq 0
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
.

(3
∘
)
For every
\mathfrak{z}\in D_{E}[0,\infty)
and every sequence
\{\mathfrak{z}_{n}\}\subset D_{E}[0,\infty)
with
\mathfrak{z}_{n}\to\mathfrak{z}
in the Skorokhod topology, the following properties hold with
{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}
-probability one:

•

For every
k\geq 1
,
\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}_{n})\to\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k}(\mathfrak{z})
as
n\to\infty
.

•

For every
k\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{z}_{n}(\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}_{n}))\to\mathfrak{z}(\tau^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}_{k}(\mathfrak{z}))
as
n\to\infty
.

•

As
n\to\infty
,
(\mathfrak{z}_{n},I^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{z}_{n}))\to(\mathfrak{z},I^{\theta,\omega^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{z}))
in the Skorokhod topology on
D_{E\times E}[0,\infty)
.

To use Lemma
4.8
in the proof of Proposition
4.6
, we take
(E,r)
to be the metric space associated with the normed space
(E_{m},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m})
, where
m\in\mathbb{N}
is chosen sufficiently large. Here,
{\mathscr{C}}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
denotes the set of continuous functions
f(x)
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
that vanish at infinity, i.e.,
\lim_{\lvert x\rvert\to\infty}f(x)=0
, and we define

\displaystyle E_{m}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\{f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2});\lVert f\rVert_{m}<\infty\},

(4.98)

where

\lVert f\rVert_{m}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\lvert f(x)\rvert(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}).

Observe that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the space
E_{m}
, endowed with the metric induced by
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
, is a Polish space. In particular, its separability follows from the Stone–Weierstrass theorem.

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
4.6
, verifying (
4.81
), (
4.84
), (
4.82
), (
4.85
), and (
4.83
) individually and in that order.

Proof of (
4.81
) in Proposition
4.6
.

It suffices to show that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle I^{4}_{n}(f,T)=-I^{3}_{n}(f,T)+\widetilde{I}^{\,3,4}_{n}(f,T),

(4.99)

where
\widetilde{I}^{\,3,4}_{n}(f,T)
denotes the term on the right-hand side of (
4.81
).

We begin by deriving an equivalent expression of
I^{4}_{n}(f,T)
.
By the definition of
I^{4}_{n}(f,T)
and the stochastic Fubini theorem,

\displaystyle I^{4}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{s=0}^{s^{\prime}}\int_{y}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t-s}(x,y){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

which is also equivalent to (
3.28
) if we set
{\varepsilon}
there to be
{\varepsilon}_{n}
. By the Chapman-Kolmogorov equation, the last equality gives

\displaystyle I^{4}_{n}(f,T)

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\int_{s=0}^{s^{\prime}}\int_{y}\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)P_{s^{\prime}-s}(z,y){\mathrm{d}}z{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl(\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)[-P_{s^{\prime}}X_{0}(z)+X_{n}(z,s^{\prime})]{\mathrm{d}}z\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)P_{s^{\prime}}X_{0}(z){\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)X_{n}(z,s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}).

Here, the second equality applies the mild form equation (
2.18
) tested against the function
z\mapsto P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)
for
s^{\prime}<t
. The final equality uses the stochastic Fubini theorem to interchange the order of integration,

{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\,f(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t=f(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}t\,{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}).

This application can be justified by Proposition
3.1
(2
∘
) and (
3.22
).

Equation (
4.99
) follows from the previous equality because the measure
X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime})
is defined to have density
X_{n}(z,s^{\prime})
with respect to Lebesgue measure, and we have

\displaystyle\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)P_{s^{\prime}}X_{0}(z){\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y^{\prime},s^{\prime},T).

This uses the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation and the definition of
\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f
in (
3.19
). The proof is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of (
4.84
) in Proposition
4.6
.

The definition (
3.19
) of
\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f
gives the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T)=\int_{t=0}^{T-s}\int_{x}f(x,t+s)P_{t+s}X_{0}(x)P_{t}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,\quad 0\leq s\leq T.

(4.100)

Since we assume
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
and bounded
X_{0}(\cdot)
, by (
7.10
), (
4.100
) implies

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T)\rvert\leq\frac{C(\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,f,m)}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{m}},\quad 0\leq s\leq T,\;y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

(4.101)

Additionally, applying Itô’s isometry yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[I^{3}_{n}(f,T)^{2}]={\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(x^{\prime},s,T)\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(x,s,T){\langle}M_{n},M_{n}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\biggr],\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[I^{3}_{\infty}(f,T)^{2}]={\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(x,s,T)^{2}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\biggr].

Consequently, (
4.84
) follows upon applying Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) for
n=\infty
.

\blacksquare

Proof of (
4.82
) in Proposition
4.6
.

First, we show that
s\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s\wedge T,T)
takes values in
C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)
, where
m\geq 10
is an arbitrary integer, and
E_{m}
is defined in (
4.98
) and equipped with the metric induced by
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
. For any fixed
0\leq s\leq T
, by substituting
x^{\prime}=x-y
in (
4.100
) and applying both (
7.10
) and the dominated convergence theorem, we see that the map
y\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T)
belongs to
E_{m}
. Thus, it remains to show that
s\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s\wedge T,T)
is continuous with respect to the norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
. To establish this, observe that by (
7.10
),

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})\int_{t=0}^{\delta}\int_{x}\frac{P_{t}(x,y)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\searrow 0,\quad\delta\searrow 0.

(4.102)

Therefore, by (
4.100
), verifying the required continuity reduces to showing
that for any
0<\delta<T
,

\displaystyle\lim_{s_{2}-s_{1}\searrow 0\atop 0\leq s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq T-\delta}\lVert\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s_{2},T)-\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s_{1},T)\rVert_{m}=0.

(4.103)

To this end, we write
\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s_{2},T)-\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s_{1},T)
as the sum of two differences using the original definition (
3.19
) of
\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s_{2},T)-\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s_{1},T)

\displaystyle\quad=\biggl(\int_{t=s_{2}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t}X_{0}(x)P_{t-s_{2}}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t=s_{2}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t}X_{0}(x)P_{t-s_{1}}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\biggl(\int_{t=s_{2}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t}X_{0}(x)P_{t-s_{1}}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{t=s_{1}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t}X_{0}(x)P_{t-s_{1}}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr).

Here, as
s_{2}-s_{1}\searrow 0
, the
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
-norm of the first difference vanishes by (
4.102
), the second bound in (
7.4
), and (
7.10
), while the
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
-norm of the second difference vanishes by (
4.102
). Therefore,
s\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s\wedge T,T)
indeed maps into
C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)
.

To prove (
4.82
), we proceed as follows. For
\theta>0
and
m\geq 10
as above, define an
E_{m}
-valued step function by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}(y,s,T)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,\tau^{\theta}_{k},T)\mathds{1}_{[\tau^{\theta}_{k},\tau^{\theta}_{k+1})}(s),

(4.104)

where
\tau^{\theta}_{k}=\tau_{k}^{\theta}(s\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(\cdot,s,T))
is as defined before Lemma
4.8
with respect to the metric space
E_{m}
. By using Markov’s inequality and Itô’s isometry, we obtain:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)-\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}-\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f)(x^{\prime},s,T)(\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}-\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f)(x,s,T)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{\theta^{2}}{(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})}\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right],

where the last inequality uses Lemma
4.8
(2
∘
) and the definition of
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
.
Hence, by Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) for
n=\infty
,

\begin{gathered}\forall\;\delta,\eta>0,\;\exists\;\theta_{0}>0\mbox{ such that }\forall\;n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\},\\
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta_{0}}(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)-\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right|>\delta\right)\leq\eta.\end{gathered}

(4.105)

Furthermore,
y\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,\tau^{\theta}_{k},T)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
by (
4.101
) for any
\theta>0
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and there is an integer
N^{\prime}\geq 1
independent of
(y,s)
such that

\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}(y,s,T)=\sum_{k=0}^{N^{\prime}}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(y,\tau^{\theta}_{k},T)\mathds{1}_{[\tau^{\theta}_{k},\tau^{\theta}_{k+1})}(s),\quad\forall\;0\leq s\leq T.

Hence, by Lemma
4.7
(4
∘
) with the function
x\mapsto\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f(x,\tau^{\theta}_{k},T)
for every
1\leq k\leq N^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}(y,s,T){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s)\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{{\mathbb{P}}}\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\mathfrak{L}_{0}^{3}f_{\theta}(y,s,T){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s),\quad\forall\;\theta>0.

(4.106)

Combining (
4.105
) and (
4.106
) proves (
4.82
).

\blacksquare

The proofs of (
4.85
) and (
4.83
) are based on Lemma
4.9
below, which provides an a priori bound.

Lemma 4.9.

Let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
p\in(0,1)
, and
f(x,t)\in\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
with
0\leq f(x,t)\leq C(f,m,T)/(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})
for some integer
m\geq 10
.
Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, any
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T
, any function
T_{3}(s)
such that
0\leq s\leq T_{3}(s)\leq T
, the following bound holds:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{s={T_{1}}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime\prime}}f(x^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},x^{\prime})\int_{z^{\prime}}P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},z^{\prime})X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime},s){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}f(x^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},x)\int_{z}P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}\biggr)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{s=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T_{3}(s)-s}\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{T_{3}(s)-s}\frac{C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p,f,m)}{\min\{t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.107)

Proof.
To prove (
4.107
), we first consider the case where
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In this situation,
we use (
3.3b
) to get the following equality:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{s={T_{1}}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime\prime}}f(x^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},x^{\prime})\int_{z^{\prime}}P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},z^{\prime})X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime},s){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}f(x^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},x)\int_{z}P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}\biggr)\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime\prime}}f(x^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime\prime})f(x^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},{\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}+x)P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},x)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl(\int_{z}\int_{z^{\prime}}P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},z^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},z){\mathbb{E}}[X_{n}(z^{\prime},s)X_{n}(z,s)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}+x,s)X_{n}(x,s)]{\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}z\biggr)\phi(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{s=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime\prime}}\frac{C(f,m,T)P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},{\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}+x)P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},x)}{(1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime\prime}\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime}\rvert^{m})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)}{(\min\{t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}-s)^{p}}\left(\int_{\overline{z}}\phi(\overline{z})\lvert\log\lvert\widetilde{x}-\overline{z}\rvert\rvert{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}+1\right)\phi(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{s=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=0}^{T_{3}(s)-s}\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=0}^{T_{3}(s)-s}\frac{C(f,m,T)(1+\lvert{\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}\rvert)}{(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)}{\min\{t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}^{p}}\left(\int_{\overline{z}}\phi(\overline{z})\lvert\log\lvert\widetilde{x}-\overline{z}\rvert\rvert{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}+1\right)\phi(\widetilde{x}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.108)

Here, the penultimate inequality follows from Theorem
3.4
(2
∘
), while (
4.108
) relies on (
7.8
) and (
7.10
). Thus, (
4.107
) holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
by (
4.108
). For
n=\infty
, the proof is straightforward since by Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) and (
4.87
), the left-hand of (
4.107
) with
n=\infty
is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{s=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{t^{\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}=s}^{T_{3}(s)}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime\prime}}\frac{C(f,m,T)P_{t^{\prime\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime\prime},x)P_{t^{\prime\prime}-s}(x^{\prime\prime},x)}{(1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime\prime}\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime}\rvert^{m})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)}{(\min\{t^{\prime\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}-s)^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s,

which can also be bounded by the right-hand side of (
4.107
) using an argument analogous to the one for deriving (
4.108
). Therefore, (
4.107
) holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

\blacksquare

Proof of (
4.85
) in Proposition
4.6
.

Recall that
I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)
is defined in Proposition
4.6
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. By Itô’s isometry,
\mathbb{E}[I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)^{2}]
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
equals the left-hand side of (
4.107
) with
T_{1}=0
,
T_{2}=T
, and
T_{3}(s)\equiv T
. The same result holds for
n=\infty
upon incorporating (
4.87
). Since the right-hand side of (
4.107
) is independent of
n
, these identities establish the required uniform bound.

\blacksquare

In the proof below, Steps 2 and 3 adapt Cho’s approach
[
25
, Section 2]
to our framework. In Cho’s original work, the weak convergence of stochastic integrals driven by martingale measures is studied as processes valued in spaces of tempered distributions.

Proof of (
4.83
) in Proposition
4.6
.

We divide the proof into four steps. Fix
T\in(0,\infty)
, let
\delta,\eta>0
denote error thresholds for probability bounds of the form
\mathbb{P}(\lvert X-Y\rvert>c\delta)\leq c\eta
, and recall that
E_{m}
is defined in (
4.98
) and equipped with the metric induced by
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
.

Step 1.
Our main goal in this step is to show two preliminary properties:

(1)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and
0<\kappa<T
, the mapping
s^{\prime}\mapsto H_{n,\kappa}(\cdot,s^{\prime})
belongs to
C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)
for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, where

H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})=H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}\wedge(T-\kappa)),\quad s^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+},

is defined by the following equation for
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa
:

\displaystyle H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{t=s^{\prime}+\kappa}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}-\kappa}\int_{x}f(x,t+s^{\prime}+\kappa)P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime}).

(4.109)

(2)

There exists some
0<\kappa_{0}<T/3
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl|I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{n,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\biggr|>\delta\biggr)\leq\eta.

(4.110)

We establish properties (1) and (2) in Steps 1-1 and 1-2 below. Step 1-3 provides a setup to connect Step 1-2 with Steps 2 and 3.

Step 1-1.
We prove property (1). The continuity of
y^{\prime}\mapsto H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})
for each fixed
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa
follows from Proposition
3.1
(2
∘
), the condition
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, and the dominated convergence theorem. The rest of property (1) will be established once we show that

\displaystyle\lim_{0\leq s_{1}^{\prime}\leq s_{2}^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa\atop s_{2}^{\prime}-s_{1}^{\prime}\searrow 0}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\lvert H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s_{1}^{\prime})-H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})=0.

(4.111)

We begin by establishing three bounds. By using (
4.109
), it is immediate that for any integer
m\geq 10
,

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{\prime}}\lvert H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s_{1}^{\prime})-H_{n,\kappa}(y^{\prime},s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{y^{\prime}}\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=T-s_{2}^{\prime}-\kappa}^{T-s^{\prime}_{1}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sup_{y^{\prime}}\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)-f(x,t+s_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sup_{y^{\prime}}\biggl|\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa)P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times[X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{2}^{\prime})](1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})\biggr|.

(4.112)

The remaining two bounds are provided by (
4.113
) and (
4.114
) as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{\prime}}P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\sup_{y^{\prime}}P_{t+\kappa}(y^{\prime})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\leq C(m,T,\kappa)(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}),\quad\forall\;0\leq t\leq T,

(4.113)

\displaystyle\int_{z}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}\biggl(\sup_{z^{\prime}}(1+\lvert z^{\prime}\rvert^{m})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z^{\prime})\biggr)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C(m,T,\kappa)\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}),

(4.114)

where the last inequality follows by (
4.113
). For the rest of Step 1-1, we show that the three terms on the right-hand side of (
4.112
