) converge uniformly to zero with respect to the mode defined in (
4.111
). The proofs proceed in the same order as these terms appear.

The first term on the right-hand side of (
4.112
) admits the bound below, which follows by applying (
4.113
) and (
4.114
):

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{\prime}}\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=T-s_{2}^{\prime}-\kappa}^{T-s^{\prime}_{1}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,T,\kappa)\int_{t=T-s_{2}^{\prime}-\kappa}^{T-s^{\prime}_{1}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s).

(4.115)

Observe that by Corollary
3.8
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T_{0}}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s)<\infty\quad\forall\;m\geq 10,\;m\in\mathbb{N},\;T_{0}\in(0,\infty).

(4.116)

Hence, the assumption that
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, the right-hand side tends to zero uniformly as
s_{2}^{\prime}-s_{1}^{\prime}\searrow 0
for
0\leq s_{1}^{\prime}\leq s_{2}^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa
.

The second term on the right-hand side of (
4.112
) can be estimated in a manner similar to the previous display:

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{\prime}}\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)-f(x,t+s_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,T,\kappa)\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{1}^{\prime}+\kappa)-f(x,t+s_{s}^{\prime}+\kappa)\rvert(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s).

(4.117)

Since
(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})f(x,t)
belongs to
{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, (
4.116
) and the assumption that
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
apply again and imply that the right-hand side also converges to zero uniformly as
s_{2}^{\prime}-s_{1}^{\prime}\searrow 0
for
0\leq s_{1}^{\prime}\leq s_{2}^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa
.

To complete the proof, we need to show that the last term in (
4.112
) approaches zero uniformly. We use the following bound for this term, with
M>0
to be sent to infinity at the end:

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{\prime}}\biggl|\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa)P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\times[X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{2}^{\prime})](1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert\leq M}\frac{C(f,m,T,\kappa)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{z}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{C(f,m,T,\kappa)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}\int_{z}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(4.118)

which follows from (
4.113
) and the assumption that
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
.
The first term on the right-hand side satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert\leq M}\frac{C(f,m,T,\kappa)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{z}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T,\kappa,M)\sup_{z}{\mathrm{e}}^{-\lvert z\rvert/[2(T+\kappa)]}\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert,

(4.119)

which uses the inequality
{\mathrm{e}}^{-\lvert x-y\rvert^{2}/a}\leq C(a){\mathrm{e}}^{-\lvert x-y\rvert/a}\leq C(a){\mathrm{e}}^{\lvert x\rvert/a-\lvert y\rvert/a}
for all
a>0
and
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. The other term satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{C(f,m,T,\kappa)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}\int_{z}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{z}\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{C(f,m,T,\kappa)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x\lvert X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T,\kappa)\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\int_{x}\frac{P_{t+\kappa}(x,z)^{2}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}{\mathrm{d}}x\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times[X_{n}(z,s_{1}^{\prime})+X_{n}(z,s_{2}^{\prime})]{\mathrm{d}}z{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T,\kappa)\biggl(\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s)\biggr),

(4.120)

where the first inequality follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the nonnegativity of
X_{n}
as stated in Proposition
3.1
(2
∘
), while the last inequality is a consequence of (
7.10
). By invoking Proposition
3.1
(2
∘
) and (
4.116
) and applying (
4.119
) and (
4.120
) to (
4.118
), we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{M\to\infty}\limsup_{0\leq s_{1}^{\prime}\leq s_{2}^{\prime}\leq T-\kappa\atop s_{2}^{\prime}-s_{1}^{\prime}\searrow 0}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\biggl|\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T-s^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{2}^{\prime}+\kappa)P_{t+\kappa}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\times[X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{1}^{\prime})-X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{2}^{\prime})](1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})\biggr|=0.

Therefore, the third term in (
4.112
) vanishes uniformly as required. This completes the proof of property (1).

Step 1-2.
We verify property (2) by establishing that (
4.110
) is satisfied for some
0<\kappa_{0}<T/3
. By Markov’s inequality, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl(I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa}\int_{y^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl(\int_{t=s^{\prime}+\kappa}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,y^{\prime})\int_{z}P_{t-s^{\prime}}(x,z)X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z,s^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\biggr)^{2}\biggr]=0.

To justify this, observe that
\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa}\int_{t=s^{\prime}+\kappa}^{T}=\int_{s^{\prime}=T-\kappa}^{T}\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{T}+\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa}\int_{t=s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}+\kappa}
. Hence, applying Itô’s isometry, Lemma
4.9
with
(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}(s^{\prime}))=(T-\kappa,T,T)
and
(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}(s^{\prime}))=(0,T-\kappa,s^{\prime}+\kappa)
, and (
4.87
) for
n=\infty

yield the result. Thus, (
4.110
) is verified.

Step 1-3.

With the choice of
\kappa_{0}
ensuring (
4.110
), we demonstrate in Steps 2 and 3 below
that there exists an integer
N_{0}\geq 1
such that for all
n\geq N_{0}
,
H_{n,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})
satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl|\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{n,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}){M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\biggr|>3\delta\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 3\eta,

(4.121)

Since we focus exclusively on
H_{n,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})
for the rest of this proof, we will simply write

H_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,H_{n,\kappa_{0}}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}),\quad n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}.

Step 2.
In this step, we show that for small enough
\theta_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\eta.

(4.122)

In (
4.122
),
H_{n}^{\theta}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})
denotes a random, function-valued step function, defined as follows. We work with
E=E_{m}
, where
(E_{m},\lvert\cdot\rvert_{m})
is given below Lemma
4.8
, and
m\geq 10
is fixed.

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\theta}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,I^{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime}\mapsto H_{n}(\cdot,s^{\prime\prime}))(s^{\prime})(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}H_{n}(y^{\prime},\tau^{\theta}_{k,n})\mathds{1}_{[\tau^{\theta}_{k,n},\tau^{\theta}_{k+1,n})}(s^{\prime}),\quad n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}.

(4.123)

Note that property (1) in Step 1 is used to justify applying Lemma
4.8
. The notation

I^{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime}\mapsto H_{n}(\cdot,s^{\prime\prime}))(s^{\prime})(y^{\prime})
refers to the value at
y^{\prime}
of the
E_{m}
-valued function
I^{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime}\mapsto H_{n}(\cdot,s^{\prime\prime}))(s^{\prime})
. Additionally,

\displaystyle\tau^{\theta}_{k,n}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\tau_{k}^{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime}\mapsto H_{n}(\cdot,s^{\prime\prime}))

(4.124)

is independent of
y^{\prime}
since its definition only uses the norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{m}
.

To prove (
4.122
), we consider the following inequalities for any
\theta>0
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{s=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}[H_{n}(x^{\prime},s)-H_{n}^{\theta}(x^{\prime},s-)][H_{n}(x,s)-H_{n}^{\theta}(x,s-)]\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{s=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{\theta^{2}}{(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})}\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right],

where the last inequality holds by using Lemma
4.8
(2
∘
) and the continuity of
s^{\prime}\mapsto H_{n}(\cdot,s^{\prime})
in
C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)
. Furthermore, Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
) for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, together with Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) and (
4.87
) for
n=\infty
, yields

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{s=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m})(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})}\mu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)\right]<\infty.

Hence, we can choose
\theta_{0}>0
small enough to attain (
4.122
).

Step 3.

This step gives the last approximation. Our goal is to show that for a.s.-
\omega
,

\displaystyle\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}).

(4.125)

Consequently, we can find an integer
N(\delta,\eta,\theta_{0},\kappa_{0})\geq 1
large enough such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})-\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\eta,\quad\forall\;n\geq N(\delta,\eta,\theta_{0},\kappa_{0}).

(4.126)

Step 3-1.
To prove (
4.125
), we need two preliminary convergence results. The first one iterates the following limit from Corollary
3.8
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\,\lim_{n\to\infty}X^{\sharp}_{n}=X^{\sharp}_{\infty}\mbox{ in $C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)$}\Bigr)=1;

(4.127)

see Corollary
3.8
. Additionally, we claim that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\,\lim_{n\to\infty}H_{n}=H_{\infty}\mbox{ in $C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)$}\Bigr)=1,\quad\forall\;m\geq 10,\;m\in\mathbb{N},

(4.128)

which is equivalent to
the condition that

\displaystyle\forall\;\mbox{ subsequence }\{n_{k}\},\;\forall\;\{s_{n_{k}}\}\subset[0,T-\kappa_{0}],

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\lvert H_{n_{k}}(y^{\prime},s_{n_{k}})-H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s_{n_{k}})\rvert(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})=0.

To confirm this, property (1) from Step 1-1 guarantees that the following condition is sufficient:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\forall\;\mbox{ subsequence }\{n_{k}\},\;\forall\;s_{n_{k}}\subset[0,T-\kappa_{0}]\mbox{ with }\lim_{k\to\infty}s_{n_{k}}=s_{\infty},\\
&\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\lvert H_{n_{k}}(y^{\prime},s_{n_{k}})-H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s_{\infty})\rvert(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})=0.\end{split}

(4.129)

To prove (
4.129
), we use (
4.109
) and (
4.116
). Hence, an inspection of the proof of (
4.112
) reveals that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\biggl|\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{n_{k}}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{n_{k}}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})\biggr|(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad=0.

(More specifically, only the analogues of the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.112
) are relevant, which means that only the analogues of (
4.115
) and (
4.117
) arise.) Consequently, (
4.129
) holds as soon as we can show that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{y^{\prime}}\biggl|\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{\infty})\biggr|(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad=0.

This, in turn, can be verified by showing that for any further subsequence, still denoted by
\{n_{k}\}
,
we have the following limit whenever
\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert\to\infty
or
y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\to y^{\prime}_{\infty}
for some
y^{\prime}_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\biggl|\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{\infty})\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad\times(1+\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert^{m})=0.

(4.130)

First, we show that (
4.130
) holds whenever
\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert\to\infty
. Let
n^{\prime}\in\{n_{k},\infty\}
, and consider
M>0
, which will ultimately be passed to infinity. Then

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})\biggr|(1+\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T)\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert\leq M}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}tX_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})(1+\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C(f,m,T)\int_{z}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\lvert x\rvert>M}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{3m}}P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}tX_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})(1+\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T,\kappa_{0},M){\mathrm{e}}^{-\lvert y^{\prime}_{n_{k}}\rvert/[2(T+\kappa_{0})]}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s)(1+\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert^{m})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C(f,m,T,\kappa_{0})\biggl(\int_{\lvert x\rvert\geq M}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{2m}}{\mathrm{d}}x\biggr)^{1/2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s),

where the first term arises from reasoning analogous to that used for (
4.119
), while the second term follows from (
4.113
) and a similar argument as in (
4.120
). Applying (
4.116
) to the right-hand side of the last inequality, we conclude that (
4.130
) holds by taking
k\to\infty
first, followed by
M\to\infty
.

Next, we show that (
4.130
) holds whenever
y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\to y^{\prime}_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. Since
\sup_{k}\lvert y^{\prime}_{n_{k}}\rvert<\infty
, it suffices to prove the following three limits, where
n^{\prime}
belongs to
\{n_{k},\infty\}
for the first limit:

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})\rvert\lvert P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})-P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{\infty}^{\prime})\rvert P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})=0,

(4.131)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{\infty}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{\infty}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{\infty})

\displaystyle\quad=0.

(4.132)

To get (
4.131
), we use (
7.3
) with the chocie of
\delta\equiv\kappa_{0}
,
{\varepsilon}\equiv\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}-y_{\infty}^{\prime}\rvert\kappa_{0}^{1/2}/(2M^{\prime})
, and
M\equiv(2M^{\prime})/\kappa_{0}^{1/2}
,
where
M^{\prime}>0
is such that
\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\rvert\leq M^{\prime}
for all
k
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})\rvert\lvert P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{n_{k}}^{\prime})-P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,y_{\infty}^{\prime})\rvert P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\kappa_{0},M^{\prime})\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}-y_{\infty}^{\prime}\rvert\int_{z}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T-s_{\infty}-\kappa_{0}}\int_{x}\lvert f(x,t+s_{\infty}+\kappa_{0})\rvert P_{2(t+\kappa_{0})}(x,y_{\infty}^{\prime})P_{t+\kappa_{0}}(x,z){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(f,m,T,\kappa_{0},M^{\prime})\lvert y_{n_{k}}^{\prime}-y_{\infty}^{\prime}\rvert\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\int_{z}\frac{1}{1+\lvert z\rvert^{m}}X_{n^{\prime}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s)\xrightarrow[k\to\infty]{}0,

where the last inequality follows by using the condition
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, as well as (
7.9
), and
the limit follows by using (
4.116
). Additionally, (
4.132
) follows directly from Corollary
3.8
, since the integrands with respect to the measures
X_{n_{k}}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{n_{k}})
and
X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,s_{\infty})
are identical and independent of
k
, and the function of
z
defining the integrands is in
{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
due to the condition
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
. This establishes (
4.130
), and therefore (
4.128
).

Step 3-2.
We present further preliminary results, listed as (A)–(D) below, to prepare the proof of (
4.125
) in this step.

(A)
We can choose an event
\Omega_{1}
of probability one on which (
3.39
), (
4.116
) and the following two limits hold:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{n\to\infty}X^{\sharp}_{n}&=X^{\sharp}_{\infty}\mbox{ in $C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)$},\\
\lim_{n\to\infty}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}&=H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}\mbox{ in }D_{E_{m}}[0,\infty),\end{split}

(4.133)

where
H_{n}^{\theta}=H_{n}^{\theta}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is the random step function defined in (
4.123
).

The existence of
\Omega_{1}
follows from the two preliminary convergence results from Step 3-1. More precisely, the second limit in (
4.133
) is ensured by (
4.128
) and Lemma
4.8
(3
∘
).
Fix
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
and, for notational simplicity, omit explicit reference to
\omega
in the rest of Step 3.

(B).
There exist strictly increasing, Lipschitz-continuous functions
\lambda_{n}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, such that all of the following limits hold:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{0}}\lvert\lambda_{n}(t)-t\rvert\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0,\quad\forall\;T_{0}\in(0,\infty),

(4.134)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{0}}\lVert H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(\cdot,\lambda_{n}(t))-H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(\cdot,t)\rVert_{m}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0,\quad\forall\;T_{0}\in(0,\infty).

(4.135)

This follows from (
4.133
) and a standard property of the Skorokhod space
[
35
, 5.3 Proposition, p.119]
.

(C).
We have

\displaystyle X_{n}^{\sharp}(\cdot,\lambda_{n}(t))\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}X^{\sharp}_{\infty}(\cdot,t)\quad\mbox{ in }C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty),

(4.136)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\lambda_{n}(t)}X^{\sharp}_{n}(\cdot,s){\mathrm{d}}s\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\int_{0}^{t}X^{\sharp}_{\infty}(\cdot,s){\mathrm{d}}s\quad\mbox{ in }C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty).

(4.137)

To establish (
4.136
), we apply (
4.134
) to the first limit in (
4.133
). Furthermore, the limit in (
4.137
) follows from (
4.134
) and (
4.136
).

(D).
We have the following two properties of the random step functions
H_{n}^{\theta_{0}}
, defined in (
4.123
),
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
:

(D-1).
For each
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, let
\{t_{k}^{n}\}=\{t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}^{n}\leq t_{2}^{n}\leq\cdots\}
, where
t_{0}^{n}=0
, and each
t_{k}^{n}
for
k\geq 1
denotes either a unique discontinuity of
H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}
or equals
\infty
. Then
\{t^{n}_{k}\}=\{\tau^{\theta_{0}}_{k,n}\}
, where
\tau^{\theta_{0}}_{k,n}
is defined in (
4.124
).

(D-2).
There exists an integer
K\geq 1
, independent of
n
, such that the number of discontinuities of
H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}
in
[0,T-\kappa_{0}]
is bounded by
K
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

To justify the identity
\{t^{n}_{k}\}=\{\tau^{\theta_{0}}_{k,n}\}
, observe that for
I^{\theta}(\mathfrak{z})
(as defined in (
4.97
)), a genuine jump necessarily occurs at every finite
\tau^{\theta}_{k}
, as such a jump must have size at least
\theta/2
with respect to the associated metric. Additionally, this observation, combined with (
4.135
) and the continuity
H_{\infty}\in C_{E_{m}}[0,\infty)
, establishes the second property listed above.

Step 3-3.

We show the required convergence in (
4.125
) with respect to the
\omega
under consideration in this step. Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{k}^{n}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\lambda_{n}^{-1}(t_{k}^{n}),\quad r_{n}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\lambda_{n}^{-1}(T-\kappa_{0}).\end{split}

(4.138)

Then the pre-limit integral in (
4.125
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}[H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))-H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})]M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})(M_{n}-M_{\infty})({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{k=0}^{K}\biggl(\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}-\int_{s^{\prime}=r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}}^{r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}}\biggr)\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{s^{\prime}=r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}}^{r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}),

(4.139)

where the second equality follows from the identity
\{t^{n}_{k}\}=\{\tau^{\theta_{0}}_{k,n}\}
in (D-1) of Step 3-2. The following list evaluates the limits of the four sums on the right-hand side as
n\to\infty
, demonstrating that the first three sums converge to zero and the last sum approaches the limit stated in (
4.125
):

•

For the first sum, we observe that

\displaystyle\Psi^{n}_{k}(y^{\prime})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))-H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})

(4.140)

(though it is a random
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
-function). The
{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
-property follows from the integral representation in (
4.109
), which establishes the
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}
-regularity. The decay property is then obtained by applying (
4.114
), (
4.116
), the condition
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, and (
7.10
).
Hence, (
3.39
) applies

\displaystyle\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}[H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))-H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})]M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=X_{n}(\Psi^{n}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}))-X_{n}(\Psi^{n}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}X_{n}\biggl(\frac{\Delta\Psi^{n}_{k}}{2},s\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.141)

We demonstrate that, for each
0\leq k\leq K
, the right-hand side of the preceding equality converges to zero as
n\to\infty
. Recall that the spatial partial derivatives of the two-dimensional heat kernel yield the Hermite polynomials. For example, the primary case of interest here is

\partial_{y_{j}}^{2}P_{t}(x,y)=\frac{(x_{j}-y_{j})^{2}-t}{t^{2}}P_{t}(x,y),

provided that
x=(x_{1},x_{2})
and
y=(y_{1},y_{2})
. Thus, employing the integral representation in (
4.109
), together with the condition
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, property (1) of Step 1 for
n=\infty
, and (
4.128
), we obtain that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lVert\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(t))-\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},t)\rVert_{m}=0

for any multi-index
\alpha
and integer
m\geq 10
.
In particular, for all
k\in\{0,\cdots,K\}
,

\lVert\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}\Psi^{n}_{k}(y^{\prime})\rVert_{m}=\lVert\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}H_{n}(y^{\prime},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))-\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})\rVert_{m}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0.

Therefore, by (
4.116
), each term on the right-hand side of (
4.141
) converges to zero as
n\to\infty
for every
0\leq k\leq K
. Since
K
is independent of
n
, the first sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) also converges to zero as
n\to\infty
.

•

For the second sum, note that by (
4.134
) and (
4.138
), for each
0\leq k\leq K
, the sequence
\{r_{n}\wedge s^{n}_{k}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded. Consequently, every subsequence admits a further subsequence, which we denote by
\{r_{n_{\ell}}\wedge s^{n_{\ell}}_{k}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
, such that

\displaystyle s^{\infty}_{k}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\lim_{\ell\to\infty}r_{n_{\ell}}\wedge s^{n_{\ell}}_{k}\quad\mbox{ exists in }[0,T-\kappa_{0}].

(4.142)

Set

\Phi^{n}_{k}(y^{\prime})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}),\quad\Phi^{\infty}_{k}(y^{\prime})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s_{k}^{\infty}),

all of which belong to
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

We now present the main argument, demonstrating that each term in the second sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) converges to zero as
n\to\infty
. By (
3.39
),

\displaystyle\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})(M_{n}-M_{\infty})({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=(X_{n}-X_{\infty})(\Phi^{n}_{k}-\Phi^{\infty}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}))-(X_{n}-X_{\infty})(\Phi^{n}_{k}-\Phi^{\infty}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}(X_{n}-X_{\infty})\biggl(\frac{\Delta(\Phi^{n}_{k}-\Phi^{\infty}_{k})}{2},s\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(X_{n}-X_{\infty})(\Phi^{\infty}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}))-(X_{n}-X_{\infty})(\Phi^{\infty}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}(X_{n}-X_{\infty})\biggl(\frac{\Delta\Phi^{\infty}_{k}}{2},s\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s.

(4.143)

Similarly to the argument for the first sum, but without applying (
4.128
),
it follows that for all
k\in\{0,\ldots,K\}
,

\lVert\partial^{\alpha}_{y^{\prime}}\Phi^{n_{\ell}}_{k}(y^{\prime})-\partial_{y^{\prime}}\Phi^{\infty}_{k}(y^{\prime})\rVert_{m}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0

for any multi-index
\alpha
and any integer
m\geq 10
. Thus, for each
0\leq k\leq K
, the first three terms on the right-hand side of (
4.141
) converge to zero by (
4.116
), while the last three terms do so by the first limit in (
4.133
). Because
\{r_{n_{\ell}}\wedge s^{n_{\ell}}_{k}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
is an arbitrary subsequence for which (
4.142
) holds, it follows that the second sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) also converges to zero as
n\to\infty
.

•

For the third sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
), we use (
3.39
) to rewrite it as follows:

\displaystyle\biggl(\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}-\int_{s^{\prime}=r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}}^{r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}}\biggr)\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=X_{\infty}(\Phi^{n}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}))-X_{\infty}(\Phi^{n}_{k},\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad-X_{\infty}(\Phi^{n}_{k},r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})+X_{\infty}(\Phi^{n}_{k},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\biggl(\int_{s^{\prime}=\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})}^{\lambda_{n}(r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n})}-\int_{s^{\prime}=r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}}^{r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}}\biggr)X_{\infty}\biggl(\frac{\Delta\Phi^{n}_{k}}{2},s\biggr){\mathrm{d}}s.

Adapting the subsequence argument previously used to establish the convergence to zero of the second sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) and substituting
\Phi^{n_{\ell}}_{k}
with
\Phi^{\infty}_{k}
, we find, using (
4.134
) as the key condition, that the third sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) also converges to zero as
n\to\infty
.

•

For the fourth sum in (
4.139
), observe that

\{t_{k}^{\infty}\wedge T\}\subset\{s_{k}^{n}\wedge T\}
for all large enough
n
, since (
4.135
) implies

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl\lVert[H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(\cdot,\lambda_{n}(t))-H^{\theta_{0}}_{n}(\cdot,\lambda_{n}(t-))]-[H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(\cdot,t)-H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(\cdot,t-)]\bigr\rVert_{m}=0.

It follows that the last sum on the right-hand side of (
4.139
) satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{s^{\prime}=r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n}}^{r_{n}\wedge s_{k+1}^{n}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H_{\infty}(y^{\prime},r_{n}\wedge s_{k}^{n})M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{r_{n}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime})\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-\kappa_{0}}\int_{y^{\prime}}H^{\theta_{0}}_{\infty}(y^{\prime},s^{\prime}-){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}s^{\prime}),

where the limit holds by (
4.134
) and the choice of
r_{n}
:
\lambda_{n}(r_{n})=T-\kappa_{0}
.

By using all of the limits we have obtained, (
4.125
) follows. We have proved (
4.125
), and thus (
4.126
).

Step 4.
From (
4.122
) and (
4.126
), we deduce (
4.121
) for all sufficiently large integers
n
. Combining (
4.110
) and (
4.121
), we conclude that for all large
n\geq 1
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lvert I^{3,4}_{n}(f,T)-I^{3,4}_{\infty}(f,T)\rvert>5\delta\right)\leq 5\eta.

This bound proves the required convergence in probability in (
4.83
).

\blacksquare

Let us close Section
4.3
with the following lemma.

Lemma 4.10.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, and
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle X_{n}(f,T)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}f(x)P_{T}X_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}P_{T-s}f(y)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s).

(4.144)

Proof.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, (
4.144
) follows directly from the weak formulation (
2.18
) and the stochastic Fubini theorem: the term
\int_{x}f(x)\int_{s=0}^{T}P_{T-s}(x,y)M_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}s){\mathrm{d}}x
exactly matches the stochastic integral in (
4.144
). Here, the justification for applying the stochastic Fubini theorem relies on the
L^{2}
condition in
[
65
, pp.296–297]
and Proposition
3.1
(1
∘
). For
n=\infty
, the result is obtained by taking the limit as
n\to\infty
in (
4.144
), similar to the proof of (
4.82
) but with a simpler argument; details are omitted.

\blacksquare

4.4
End of the proofs of Theorem
2.7
(1
∘
) and Theorem
2.10

To conclude the proof of Theorem
2.7
(1
∘
), we apply Theorem
3.5
for tightness and Lemma
4.10
to verify (
2.23
). Additionally, Tsai’s theorem
[
64
]
ensures that
X_{\varepsilon}
admits a unique distributional limit as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
. The next proposition summarizes the unique characterizations of the remaining limits in Theorem
3.5
, as well as that of the martingale measure
M_{\infty}
initially defined in (
3.39
). Specifically, it restates (
4.19
), (
4.20
), and (
4.87
), and refers to Lemma
4.7
(2
∘
)–(4
∘
) for extensions of
M_{\infty}
to more general test functions. For future reference, these characterizations are presented in the framework of Section
3.3
, independently of Tsai’s theorem.

Proposition 4.11.

The martingale measure
M_{\infty}
and the measures
\nu_{\infty}
,
\mu_{\infty}
, and
\mathring{\mu}_{\infty}
are each uniquely determined by
X_{\infty}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}g(x^{\prime},x,t)\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}g(x,x,t){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t),

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}g(x,x,t)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle=\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}g(x^{\prime},x,t)\mu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t),

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}g(x^{\prime},x,t)\mathring{\mu}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{x^{\prime}}g(x^{\prime},x,t)\phi(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\right)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t),

which holds for all
g(x^{\prime},x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
.

Proof of Theorem
2.10
.

Let
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
. By Propositions
4.1
,
4.3
, and
4.6
, the limits of the three terms
I^{1}_{n}(f,T)
,
I^{2}_{n}(f,T)
, and
I^{3}_{n}(f,T)+I^{4}_{n}(f,T)
as
n\to\infty
are determined. By applying these limits to (
3.12
), and using
\mathfrak{L}_{0}f(x,t,T)
as defined in (
4.1
) and the notation
[\cdot]_{\times}
from (
2.41
), we obtain the following equation:

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)[P_{t}X_{0}(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}_{0}f(x,t,T)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)+\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\biggl(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})(\log\rvert y^{\prime\prime}-y\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})(\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}\biggr)f(x,t)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\int_{z_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-t^{\prime}}(x,z_{2})\\
P_{t-t^{\prime}}(x,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}).

(4.145)

Because
\phi
is a probability density by (
3.3b
), the first two integrals on the right-hand side combine to give
\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)\,\nu_{\infty}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}t)
, where
\mathfrak{L}
is defined in (
2.28
). Since
\langle M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}=\nu_{\infty}
, rearranging (
4.145
) completes the proof of Theorem
2.10
, where the notation
(X,M)
is now used for the unique limit, as ensured by Theorem
2.7
(1
∘
).

\blacksquare

5
Formulas for the limiting covariation measures

5.1
Solutions to the integro-multiplication equations

In this subsection, we establish Lemma
5.1
, with its proof deferred to the end of the section. At this stage, (
2.29
) can only be justified by restricting to special test functions. We subsequently demonstrate in Section
5.2
how to extend this result to all formulas in Theorem
2.10
.

Lemma 5.1.

For any nonnegative
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and
T\in(0,\infty)
, the following formula holds:

\displaystyle\forall\;0\leq S\leq T,\quad

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}\langle M_{\infty},M_{\infty}\rangle({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\int_{z_{2}}[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t-t^{\prime}}(x;z_{1},z_{2})X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}),

(5.1)

where
\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}(x;z_{1},z_{2})
and
\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}F(x)
are defined as in (
1.15
).

The following theorem is a crucial step in proving Lemma
5.1
and may also be of independent interest. In light of Theorem
2.10
, we focus on solving the equation
\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)=[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}
, where
\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)
is defined as in (
2.28
) and is independent of the initial condition
X_{0}(\cdot)
. Note that the integral term in
\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)
is well-defined only under sufficient regularity on
f
, such as the
{\mathscr{C}}^{1}
-regularity required in Theorem
2.7
; recall Lemma
4.2
(1
∘
) for details.

Theorem 5.2.

For any fixed
T\in(0,\infty)
and any nonnegative
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, there exists a solution
f(x,t)
to the following problem:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mbox{$\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)$ is well-defined}&\mbox{$\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}$ and $0\leq t<T$},\\
\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)=[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}&\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mbox{ and }0\leq t<T,\end{cases}

(5.2)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}f(x,t,T)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\biggl(\frac{\log(T-t)}{2}+\log 2+\lambda-\frac{\gamma_{\sf EM}}{2}-\int_{y^{\prime}}\int_{y}\phi(y^{\prime})\phi(y)\log\rvert y^{\prime}-y\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\biggr)f(x,t)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{s^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}[f(x-x^{\prime},t+t^{\prime})-f(x,t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

(5.3)

Specifically, the problem stated in (
5.2
) admits a solution given by

f(x,t)\equiv\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T]),

where, with
g_{0}^{\otimes 2}(x^{\prime},x)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,g_{0}(x^{\prime})g_{0}(x)
,
\mathfrak{m}_{g_{0}}^{T}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{g_{0}}(x,t)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\;\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}g_{0}^{\otimes 2}(x),

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g_{0}}(x,t)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\mathfrak{m}_{g_{0}}(x,T-t),\quad 0\leq t\leq T.

(5.5)

Moreover, there exists a sequence
\{f_{n}(x,t)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
such that for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} f_{n}(x,t)&\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,T-t),&\lvert f_{n}(x,t)\rvert&\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}},\\
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)&\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\mathfrak{[}P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2},&\lvert\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)\rvert&\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}},\end{aligned}

(5.6)

all of which hold pointwise in
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
.

Remark 5.3.

(1
∘
) For any bounded
F
, the function
\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}F(\cdot)
defined in (
1.15
) obeys the following uniform bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\lvert\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}F(x)\rvert\leq\frac{C(F,T,\beta)}{\log(t\wedge\tfrac{1}{2})^{-1}},\quad\forall\;0<t\leq T<\infty.

(5.7)

This result follows because

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t)\leq\frac{C(\beta,T)}{t\log^{2}(t\wedge\tfrac{1}{2})},\quad 0<t\leq T<\infty;

(5.8)

see
[
19
, Proposition 4.5]
. We also use the identity
\int{\mathrm{d}}t/(t\log^{2}t)=-1/(\log t)+C
for all
0<t<1
.

(2
∘
) The second condition in (
5.2
) indicates that
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
converges to
g(x)^{2}
as
t\nearrow T
. Alternatively, this result follows from the following calculation:

\displaystyle\lim_{s\nearrow T}\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\nearrow T}-\frac{\log(T-t)}{4\pi}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)=\lim_{t\searrow 0}-\frac{\log t}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau g(x)^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\searrow 0}-\log t\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\beta^{u}t^{u}}{\Gamma(u+1)}{\mathrm{d}}ug(x)^{2}=g(x)^{2}.

Here, the first equality results from combining the observation in (1
∘
) with Lemma
5.6
(2
∘
), the third equality applies the formula (
1.16
) for
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\cdot)
, and the final equality uses the identity
\int t^{u}{\mathrm{d}}u=t^{u}/\log t+C
for all
0<t\neq 1
. Additionally, examining the derivation of the first and second equalities shows that these limits hold uniformly for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

\blacklozenge

Our proof of Theorem
5.2
is grounded in Proposition
5.4
below, combined with essential regularity properties from Lemma
5.6
. Roughly speaking,
rather than treating (
5.2
) as a purely analytic problem, our argument utilizes established formulas for the limiting moments of the approximate SHE (
1.4
), evaluated with the initial condition
g
specified in Theorem
5.2
. We denote the corresponding approximate solutions by
\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}
, with the tilde notation extending analogously to all related objects. As in Corollary
3.6
, we may assume that there exists a sequence
\{{\varepsilon}_{n}\}\subset(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]
with
{\varepsilon}_{n}\searrow 0
such that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{X}_{n},\widetilde{\nu}_{n})\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(\widetilde{X}_{{\varepsilon}_{n}},\widetilde{\nu}_{{\varepsilon}_{n}})\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}(\widetilde{X}_{\infty},\widetilde{\nu}_{\infty})\mbox{ in $C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)\times C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)$ a.s.},

(5.9)

where
\widetilde{\nu}_{\varepsilon}
is defined as in (
3.35b
).

Proposition 5.4.

Let
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be nonnegative, and let
T\in(0,\infty)
.

(1
∘
)
For all
\widetilde{f}(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
,

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{f}(x,t,T)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)[P_{t}g(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s.

(5.10)

(2
∘
)
For all
\widetilde{f}(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
,

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{f}(x,t,T)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,T-t,T){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.11)

where
\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}
is as defined in (
5.5
).

(3
∘
)
For every
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
0\leq t<T
, the following identity holds:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)=[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2},

(5.12)

where
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
is well defined.

Proposition
5.4
(3
∘
) demonstrates that
\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}
solves the problem in (
5.2
), establishing the first claim of Theorem
5.2
. The weak formulation of (
5.12
) follows directly by comparing the right-hand sides of (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) from Proposition
5.4
(1
∘
) and (2
∘
). Notably, (
5.11
) links
\mathfrak{m}_{g}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}
through
\mathfrak{L}
, reflecting a time reversal perspective. A similar property arises in the definition of
\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}F(x)
in (
1.15
), with further clarification provided in (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) below.

Remark 5.5.

When
\widetilde{f}(x,t)\equiv\mathfrak{m}_{h}^{T}(x,t)
for nonnegative
h\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, (
5.11
) follows directly from (
5.10
). Specifically, the definitions in (
1.15
), (
5.4
), and (
5.5
) imply

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{h}(x,t)[P_{t}g(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}_{h}(x,T-t)[P_{t}g(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.13)

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)[P_{t}h(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,T-t)[P_{t}h(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.14)

Hence, by (
5.10
),

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{h}(x,t,T)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}_{h}(x,t)\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.15)

which in turn is equivalent to

\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{h}(x,t,T)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{h}(x,t)\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,T-t,T){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

as claimed above. In more detail, although we do not directly verify that
(x,t)\mapsto\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}(x,t)
and
(x,t)\mapsto\mathfrak{m}_{h}^{T}(x,t)
belong to
{\mathscr{C}}^{1}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, Lemma
5.6
below ensures that (
5.10
) still holds when
\widetilde{f}(x,t)
is taken as either of these functions. Consequently, (
5.15
) is valid.
\blacklozenge

Proof of Proposition
5.4
(1
∘
).

We apply the analogue of (
2.27
) for
\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}
. Specifically, one side of this equation yields the following expectation:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t}X_{0}(x)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{y}\biggl(\int_{s=t}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,s)\int_{z}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t}(x,y)\\
P_{s-t}(x,z)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}t)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)[P_{t}X_{0}(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.16)

where the equality holds because (
4.85
) ensures that the following process is a martingale:

\int_{t=0}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{y}\biggl(\int_{s=t}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,s)\int_{z}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t}(x,y)\\
P_{s-t}(x,z)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}t),\quad 0\leq t^{\prime}\leq T.

By using the analogue of (
2.27
) for
\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}
, the expectation on the left-hand side of (
5.16
) also equals the next expectation:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{f}(x,t,T){\langle}\widetilde{M}_{\infty},\widetilde{M}_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)\right]=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\widetilde{f}(x,t,T)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.17)

where the equality holds by Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) and (
4.3
). Combining (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) proves Proposition
5.4
(1
∘
).

\blacksquare

To prove the remainder of Proposition
5.4
, parts (2
∘
) and (3
∘
), we require the following lemma. This lemma establishes regularity properties of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}
and ensures that
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}
, particularly its integral part, is well defined.

Lemma 5.6.

Let
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be noonnegative, and let
T\in(0,\infty)
.

(1
∘
)
For all
x,x^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, all
t^{\prime},t\in[0,T]
, and all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(m,g,T)\left(\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m+1}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\right)\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t+t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=t}^{t+t^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\sqrt{t^{\prime}}.

(5.19)

(2
∘
)
The integral part of
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
, with
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
0\leq t<T
, is absolutely convergent and satisfies the following bound for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\lvert\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t+t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}(x,t)\rvert P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}.

(5.20)

Hence,
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
is well-defined for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
0\leq t<T
.

(3
∘
)
The function
(x,t)\mapsto\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
is continuous on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
and admits a unique continuous extension to
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{T\}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.6
(1
∘
).

To prove (
5.18
), recall the definition (
5.4
) of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}
, and consider the following expression, which follows from substitutions:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(x-x^{\prime}+y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-\tau}(x-x^{\prime}+y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(x+y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-\tau}(x+y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau.

(5.21)

To bound the right-hand side, let
\{\mathcal{W}_{s}\}
denote a four-dimensional Brownian motion with zero initial condition. Observe that for any
G\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})
and constants
A_{0},A_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
, the mean-value theorem gives, for all
0<s\leq T
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\lvert G(A_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{s})-G(A_{1}+\mathcal{W}_{s})\rvert]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\lvert A_{0}-A_{1}\rvert\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\lvert\nabla G(aA_{0}+(1-a)A_{1}+\mathcal{W}_{s})\rvert\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,G)\lvert A_{0}-A_{1}\rvert{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\,\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\frac{1}{1+\lvert aA_{0}+(1-a)A_{1}+\mathcal{W}_{s}\rvert^{m}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,G)\lvert A_{0}-A_{1}\rvert{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\,\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\frac{1+\lvert\mathcal{W}_{s}\rvert^{m}}{1+\lvert aA_{0}+(1-a)A_{1}\rvert^{m}}\,\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,G,T)\lvert A_{0}-A_{1}\rvert\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\left(\frac{1}{1+\lvert aA_{0}+(1-a)A_{1}\rvert^{m}}\right),

(5.22)

where the third equality uses (
7.8
), and the fourth equality follows since, by Doob’s inequality,
{\mathbb{E}}[\max_{0\leq r\leq 1}\lvert W_{r}\rvert^{p}]<\infty
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
for a one-dimensional Brownian motion
W
. By applying (
5.22
) with the choice of
s\equiv t-\tau
,
A_{0}\equiv(x-x^{\prime}+y,x-x^{\prime}+y)
,
A_{1}\equiv(x+y,x+y)
, and
G(z_{1},z_{2})\equiv g(z_{1})g(z_{2})
to (
5.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\forall\;0\leq t\leq T,\quad

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T)\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert\left(\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x-ax^{\prime}+y\rvert^{m}}\right){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T)\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert\left(\frac{1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m}+\lvert y\rvert^{m}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\right){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T)\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\left(\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m+1}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\right),

where the second inequality uses (
7.8
), as well as the inequality
(b+c)^{m}\leq C(m)(b^{m}+c^{m})
for all
b,c\geq 0
. The last inequality proves (
5.18
).

To prove (
5.19
), we consider the following difference for all
t^{\prime},t\in[0,T]
, using the definition (
5.4
) of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t+t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\tau=0}^{t+t^{\prime}}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad=\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},1}+\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},2},

(5.23)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},1}

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{\tau=0}^{t+t^{\prime}}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},2}

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-\tau}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}g(z_{2})\\
g(z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau.

The two differences in (
5.23
) can be bounded as follows. First,
it follows from (
7.10
) that

\displaystyle\lvert\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},1}\rvert\leq\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=t}^{t+t^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau,\quad\forall\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

(5.24)

To bound
\Delta_{\ref{mgtdiff},2}
, we employ an argument similar to (
5.22
), thus obtaining

\displaystyle\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[G(A_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{t+t^{\prime}-\tau})-G(A_{0}+\mathcal{W}_{t-\tau})\bigr]\bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[\bigl|G(A_{0}+\sqrt{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}\mathcal{W}_{1})-G(A_{0}+\sqrt{t-\tau}\mathcal{W}_{1})\bigr|\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sqrt{t^{\prime}}\lvert\mathcal{W}_{1}\rvert\sup_{0\leq a\leq 1}\bigl|\nabla G\bigl(A_{0}+(a\sqrt{t+t^{\prime}-\tau}+(1-a)\sqrt{t-\tau})\mathcal{W}_{1}\bigr)\bigr|\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,G,T)\sqrt{t^{\prime}}}{1+\lvert A_{0}\rvert^{m}},\quad\forall\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},

(5.25)

where the second inequality uses the inequality
\lvert\sqrt{b}-\sqrt{c}\rvert\leq\sqrt{b-c}
for all
b\geq c\geq 0
. By applying (
5.24
), (
5.25
) and (
7.10
) to (
5.23
) and choosing
A_{0}\equiv(y,y)
and
G(z_{1},z_{2})\equiv g(z_{1})g(z_{2})
for (
5.25
), we establish the following inequality, thereby proving (
5.19
):

\displaystyle\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t+t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=t}^{t+t^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\sqrt{t^{\prime}},\quad\forall\;t^{\prime},t\in[0,T],\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

The proof of Lemma
5.6
(1
∘
) is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
5.6
(2
∘
).

We begin by deriving a bound that features the multiplicative factor
(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})^{-1}
, as appears on the right-hand side of (
5.20
). According to the definition (
5.5
) of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}
, the left-hand side of (
5.20
) can be expressed as the following integral:

\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},T-t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,T-t)\rvert P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

We can bound this integral by setting
S=T-t
. Specifically, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
0<S\leq T
,

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},S-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,S)\rvert P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\biggl[C(m,g,T)\left(\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{m+1}}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\right)\int_{\tau=0}^{T}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\biggr]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\biggl[\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\frac{C(m,g,T)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{\tau=0}^{T}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\sqrt{t^{\prime}}\biggr]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},

where the final inequality uses (
5.18
) and (
5.19
). Therefore, by (
5.8
) and (
7.9
),

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\lvert\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},S-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,S)\rvert P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\left(1+\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\right),\quad 0<S\leq T,

which establishes the required bound involving
(1+\lvert x\rvert^{m})^{-1}
.

To complete the proof of (
5.20
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\leq C(T,\beta),\quad\forall\;0<S\leq T<\infty.

(5.26)

We consider
S\geq 1/4
and
0<S<1/4
separately. First, we perform the following calculation:

\displaystyle\forall\;S:0<S\leq T\;\&\;S\geq 1/4,

\displaystyle\quad\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}}{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{1/8}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}}{t^{\prime}}+\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime}=1/8}^{S}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}}{t^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggl(\int_{\tau=0}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\max_{1/8\leq\tau\leq S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)+\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime}=1/8}^{S}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}}{t^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggl(\int_{\tau=0}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq C(T,\beta),

(5.27)

where the third inequality follows since
S-t^{\prime}\geq 1/8
for all
S\geq 1/4
and

0\leq t^{\prime}\leq 1/8
, and the last inequality holds by (
5.8
). To handle the case of
0<S<1/4
, we use
(
5.8
). Hence,

\displaystyle\forall\;0<S<1/4,\quad

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{\tau=S-t^{\prime}}^{S}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\beta)\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\frac{\log S-\log(S-t^{\prime})}{(\log S)\log(S-t^{\prime})}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}}{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=C(\beta)\int_{x=0}^{1}\frac{\log S-\log(S-Sx)}{(\log S)\log(S-Sx)}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{x}\quad\mbox{($\because x=t^{\prime}/S$)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{-\log S}\int_{x=0}^{3/4}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}x}{-\log S-\log(1-x)}+\frac{C(\beta)}{-\log S}\int_{x=3/4}^{1}\frac{-\log(1-x){\mathrm{d}}x}{-\log S-\log(1-x)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\beta)}{\log^{2}S}+\frac{C(\beta)}{-\log S}.

(5.28)

Here, the second inequality follows since both
-\log S
and
-\log(1-x)
are positive, and to obtain the first term on the right-hand side of the same inequality, we also use the fact that
\lim_{x\searrow 0}-x^{-1}\log(1-x)=1
. Combining (
5.27
) and (
5.28
) proves (
5.26
). The proof of (2
∘
) is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
5.6
(3
∘
).

By Remark
5.3
(2
∘
), it suffices to prove that the mapping
(x,s)\mapsto\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,s,T)
is continuous on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
. By using (
5.18
) and (
5.19
), this task reduces to proving that the following mapping is continuous on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times(0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle(x,S)\mapsto\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},S-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,S)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

(5.29)

To address this property, we consider the following equation for
\widehat{x},\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
\widehat{S},\widetilde{S}\in(0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x}-x^{\prime},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x}-x^{\prime},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x}-x^{\prime},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x}-x^{\prime},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

(5.30)

Here, splitting the two singular integrals on the left-hand side is justified for two reasons:
(1) Lemma
5.6
(2
∘
) establishes their absolute convergence; (2) the first and third integrals on the right-hand side are also absolutely convergent by (
5.18
) and (
7.9
).

Part (2) of the above justification of splitting singular integrals extends to the continuity of those integrals on the right-hand side. Specifically, by (
5.18
) with
m=0
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\leq\widetilde{S}\}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x}-x^{\prime},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})]+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\leq\widehat{S}\}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x}-x^{\prime},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})]\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(g,\overline{S},\beta)\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert,\quad\forall\;\widehat{S},\widetilde{S}\in(0,\overline{S}],\;0<\overline{S}<\infty,

and by (
7.9
),
\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\overline{S}}\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}<\infty
. Hence, by dominated convergence and the continuity of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, the following limit holds for every
\overline{S}\in(0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle\lim_{\lvert\widetilde{x}-\widehat{x}\rvert\to 0\atop\widetilde{S}-\widehat{S}\searrow 0,\;\widehat{S},\widetilde{S}\in(0,\overline{S}]}\biggl|\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x}-x^{\prime},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x}-x^{\prime},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\biggr|=0.

(5.31)

The remainder of the right-hand side of (
5.30
) is given by the following difference:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

To show that this difference converges to zero as
\lvert\widetilde{x}-\widehat{x}\rvert\to 0
and
\widetilde{S}-\widehat{S}\searrow 0
, we use a modified version of the derivation in (
5.28
) to obtain a dominating bound. For all
0<S_{0}<1/2<S_{1}<\infty
, with
\widehat{S},\widetilde{S}\in(S_{0},S_{1})
and
0<t^{\prime}<S_{1}
, (
5.19
) with
m=0
yields:

\displaystyle\Bigl|[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widetilde{S}\}}-[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widehat{S}\}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(g,S_{1})\int_{\tau=\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{S}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widetilde{S}\}}+C(g,S_{1})\int_{\tau=\widehat{S}-t^{\prime}}^{\widehat{S}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widehat{S}\}}+C(g,S_{1},\beta)\sqrt{t^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(g,S_{0},S_{1},\beta)\biggl[\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2\}}\biggl(-\log\biggl(1-\frac{t^{\prime}}{\widetilde{S}}\biggr)\biggr)+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2\}}\biggl(-\log\biggl(1-\frac{t^{\prime}}{\widehat{S}}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+t^{\prime}+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2\}}\int_{\tau=0}^{S_{1}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\sqrt{t^{\prime}}\biggr].

(5.32)

Here, the last inequality justified as follows: Let
S^{\prime}\in\{\widetilde{S},\widehat{S}\}
, and write

\displaystyle\{t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}\}=\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2,t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}<3/8\}\cup\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2,t^{\prime}<S^{\prime},S^{\prime}\geq 3/8\}\cup\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2,t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}\}.

By splitting the next integral according to this decomposition of
\{t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}\}
, we find that

\displaystyle\int_{\tau=S^{\prime}-t^{\prime}}^{S^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}\}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\tau=S^{\prime}-t^{\prime}}^{S^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2,t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}<3/8\}}+C(\beta^{\prime},S_{0},S_{1})t^{\prime}+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2\}}\int_{\tau=0}^{S_{1}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\beta,S_{1})\frac{\log S^{\prime}-\log(S^{\prime}-t^{\prime})}{(\log S^{\prime})\log(S^{\prime}-t^{\prime})}\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2,t^{\prime}<S^{\prime}<3/8\}}+C(\beta,S_{0},S_{1})t^{\prime}+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2\}}\int_{\tau=0}^{S_{1}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\beta,S_{0},S_{1})\biggl(-\log\biggl(1-\frac{t^{\prime}}{S^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggr)\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2\}}+C(\beta,S_{0},S_{1})t^{\prime}+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2\}}\int_{\tau=0}^{S_{1}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau,

where the first term on the right-hand side of the second inequality is obtained from (
5.8
). The last inequality is sufficient to justify (
5.32
). Furthermore, by monotonicity, (
5.32
) gives the following bound, which is the dominating bound we need:

\displaystyle\Bigl|[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widetilde{S}\}}-[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<\widehat{S}\}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(g,\beta,S_{0},S_{1})\biggl[\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}<S_{0}/2\}}\left(-\log\left(1-\frac{t^{\prime}}{S_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+t^{\prime}+\mathds{1}_{\{t^{\prime}\geq S_{0}/2\}}\int_{\tau=0}^{S_{1}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau+\sqrt{t^{\prime}}\biggr].

(5.33)

To complete the proof, note that the right-hand side of (
5.33
), viewed as a function of
t^{\prime}
, lies in
L^{1}((0,S_{1}),(1/t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})
because
\lim_{x\searrow 0}-x^{-1}\log(1-x)=1
. Therefore, by dominated convergence and the continuity of the mapping
(x,t)\mapsto\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
, the following limit holds for any
0<S_{0}<S_{1}<\infty
:

\displaystyle\lim_{\lvert\widetilde{x}-\widehat{x}\rvert\to 0\atop\widetilde{S}-\widehat{S}\searrow 0,\;\widehat{S},\widetilde{S}\in(S_{0},S_{1})}\biggl|\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widetilde{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{\widehat{S}}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S}-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widehat{x},\widehat{S})]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\biggr|=0.

(5.34)

Finally, applying (
5.31
) and (
5.34
) to (
5.30
) proves the required continuity of the mapping in (
5.29
). The proof of Lemma
5.6
(3
∘
) is complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of Proposition
5.4
(2
∘
).

Set

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}[\widetilde{f}(x-x^{\prime},t+t^{\prime})-\widetilde{f}(x,t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.35)

By the definition (
5.3
) of
\mathfrak{L}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\biggl(\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\log t-\log(T-t)}{4\pi}\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.36)

More precisely, this equation suffices because, by the definition (
5.5
) of
\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)
, the right-hand side of (
5.35
) equals

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\biggl(\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-(T-t)}[\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},T-t+t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,T-t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\log[T-(T-t)]}{4\pi}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,T-t)-\frac{\log(T-t)}{4\pi}\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Thus, by rearranging, (
5.36
) is sufficient to recover (
5.11
). Furthermore, by (
4.3
) and Lemma
5.6
(2
∘
), both sides of (
5.11
) are absolutely convergent integrals and therefore well-defined. In the argument below, we establish (
5.36
) by a series of rearrangements and substitutions, using Fubini’s theorem repeatedly. The need for these multiple arrangements also arises from the justification of Fubini’s theorem.

Step 1.
We claim that

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{x}[\widetilde{f}(x-x^{\prime},t^{\prime})-\widetilde{f}(x,t)]P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.37)

To justify this, observe that by (
4.3
), Fubini’s theorem allows us to interchange the order of integration on the right-hand side of (
5.36
):
\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}
becomes
\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{x}
. Performing a substitution for
t^{\prime}
then yields (
5.37
).

Step 2.
We claim that (
5.37
) implies

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})[\widetilde{f}(x,t^{\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t)-\widetilde{f}(x,t)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.38)

To justify this, we use the fact that for all
0<t<t^{\prime}<T
,

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x-x^{\prime},t^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}
