\displaystyle\quad=\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\widetilde{f}(\widetilde{x},t^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(\widetilde{x}-x^{\prime\prime},t){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}\quad(\because\widetilde{x}=x-x^{\prime},\;x^{\prime\prime}=-x^{\prime}).

Since the integral on the right-hand side and

\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

are both absolutely convergent, (
5.38
) follows upon using the right-hand side of (
5.37
).

Step 3.
We claim that (
5.38
) implies

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\widetilde{f}(x,t^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(5.39)

To justify this, observe that the integral on the right-hand side of (
5.38
) is absolutely convergent. This follows by expressing

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(x,t^{\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t)-\widetilde{f}(x,t)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)

\displaystyle\quad=[\widetilde{f}(x,t^{\prime})-\widetilde{f}(x,t)]\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t)+\widetilde{f}(x,t)[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)].

Here, the first term permits the use of (
7.9
), since
\widetilde{f}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T])
by assumption. The second term similarly allows applications of both (
5.18
) and (
7.9
). Absolute convergence of the integral on the right-hand side of (
5.38
) justifies invoking Fubini’s theorem to switch the order of integration:
\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}
becomes
\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}
. Finally, substituting for
t^{\prime}
, (
5.38
) yields (
5.39
).

Step 4.

We perform the following calculation, where the first equality follows by applying Fubini’s theorem to interchange the order of integration on the right-hand side of (
5.39
) so that
\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T}
becomes
\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{t=0}^{t^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{t=0}^{t^{\prime}}\widetilde{f}(x,t^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}-t}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t){\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t-t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime\prime},t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)]{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T-t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x,

(5.40)

where the last equality used the convention that
\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T-t}=-\int_{t^{\prime}=T-t}^{t}
when
T-t<t
.

Step 5.
The final step is to establish the following equivalent form of (
5.36
), which completes the present proof:

\displaystyle I_{\ref{def:dualeq}}

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime},t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\left(\frac{\log t-\log(T-t)}{4\pi}\right)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.41)

To obtain this equation, we consider (
5.40
) and observe that

\displaystyle\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T-t}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(T-t)-\log t}{4\pi}.

(5.42)

Therefore, the following integral is absolutely convergent, and the subsequent equality is justified by Fubini’s theorem:

\displaystyle\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=t}^{T-t}\widetilde{f}(x,t)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime})\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\widetilde{f}(x,t)\left(\frac{\log(T-t)-\log t}{4\pi}\right)\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,t){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.43)

Since the left-hand side of (
5.43
) is absolutely convergent, the required equation (
5.41
) follows upon applying (
5.43
) to (
5.40
).

\blacksquare

Proof of Propostion
5.4
(4
∘
).

The required identity is obtained by comparing the right-hand sides of (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), with Lemma
5.6
(3
∘
) providing the reinforcement needed to establish the identity pointwise.

\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

The first part of this theorem, concerning the solution to the problem in (
5.2
), has already been established in Proposition
5.4
(3
∘
). Therefore, it remains to construct a sequence
\{f_{n}(x,t)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T])
such that (
5.6
) holds pointwise for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T)
and any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. To define these functions, let
\varrho_{\rm space}(y)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\varrho_{\rm time}(r)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be probability densities supported on
\lvert y\rvert<1
and
\lvert r\rvert<1
, respectively. Then define

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,n^{2}\varrho_{\rm space}(ny)n\varrho_{\rm time}(nr){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}r,\quad(y,r)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}},\;n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle f_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{r}\int_{y}\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r),\quad 0\leq t\leq T.

It is standard to verify that
f_{n}(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T])
, and that
f_{n}(x,t)
converges to
\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x,T-t)
for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,T]
. Furthermore, applying (
5.19
) with
t=0
, along with (
5.8
) and (
7.10
), demonstrates that
f_{n}
satisfies the first bound in (
5.6
). The following Steps 1–4 establish the two properties of
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)
claimed in (
5.6
), assuming
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
.

Step 1.
We first write out the expression for
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)
by applying (
5.3
): for every
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\biggl(\frac{\log(T-t)}{2}+\log 2+\lambda-\frac{\gamma_{\sf EM}}{2}-\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime}}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})\phi(y^{\prime})\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}-y^{\prime}\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{r}\int_{y}\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\int_{r}\int_{y}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

(5.44)

The next two steps analyze two particular parts of the right-hand side of (
5.44
).

Step 2.

We start by considering the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.44
), treating the cases
r<0
and
r\geq 0
separately. The argument uses the fact that whenever
T+r-t>0
and
\lvert r\rvert<1
, Lemma
5.6
(2
∘
) applies. Together with (
7.8
), this yields the following inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T+r-t}\bigl|\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime}-y,T+r-t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-y,T+r-t)\bigr|P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}.

(5.45)

We now address the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.44
) as follows.
Whenever
-1<r<0
and
T+r-t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T+r-t}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,T+r-t-t^{\prime}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,T+r-t\big)\bigr]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=T+r-t}^{T-t}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,0\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,T+r-t\big)\bigr]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

(5.46)

The two integrals on the right-hand side can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T+r-t}\bigl|\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,T+r-t-t^{\prime}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,T+r-t\big)\bigr|P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}},

(5.47)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=T+r-t}^{T-t}\bigl|\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,0\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,T+r-t\big)\bigr|P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T,\beta)\frac{\log(T-t)-\log(T+r-t)}{(1+\lvert x-y\rvert^{m})\log[(T+r-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}

(5.48)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}.

(5.49)

Here, (
5.47
) follows from (
5.45
), and since
\mathfrak{m}_{g}(y,0)\equiv 0
, (
5.48
) is obtained by modifying the proof of (
5.7
), specifically by invoking (
1.15
), (
5.8
) and (
7.10
). Furthermore, (
5.49
) follows from the assumption
r<0
and the bound in (
7.8
). Additionally, whenever
0\leq r<1
, (
5.45
) directly gives

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\bigl|\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr|P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}.

(5.50)

We note, for later use, that whenever
0\leq r<1
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=T-t}^{T-t+r}\bigl|\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr|P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T,\beta)\min\left\{\frac{(1+\lvert y\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}},\frac{\log(T+r-t)-\log(T-t)}{(1+\lvert x-y\rvert^{m})\log[(T+r-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}\right\}.

(5.51)

This follows from (
5.45
) and a modification of the argument used to derive (
5.48
), except that now bounding both of
\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x’-y,(T+r-t-t’)^{+}\big)
and
\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)
.

Step 3.

We analyze the singular part of the remainder on the right-hand side of (
5.44
). Its absolute value equals the left-hand side of the first inequality below, while the subsequent inequality follows by an argument similar to that used for (
5.48
): for
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
,

\displaystyle\left|-\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\log(T-t)}{2}\int_{r}\int_{y}\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{r}\int_{y}\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)\lvert\log(T-t)\rvert}{(1+\lvert x-y\rvert^{m})\log[(T+r-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(m,g,T,\beta)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\lvert\log(T-t)\rvert}{\log[(T+r/n-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}\varrho_{\rm time}(r){\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T,\beta)\frac{\lvert\log(T-t)\rvert}{\log[(T+1-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(m,g,T,\beta).

(5.52)

Here, the second inequality follows from (
7.10
) and a substitution, while the third relies on the fact that
a\mapsto\log(a\wedge\frac{1}{2})^{-1}
is decreasing for
a>0
.

Step 4.
In this step, we show that
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t)
satisfies the limit and bound required in (
5.6
). By using (
5.19
), (
5.46
), (
5.47
), (
5.49
), (
5.50
) and (
5.52
), we obtain the necessary bound in (
5.6
). Additionally, (
5.46
), (
5.47
), (
5.49
) and (
5.50
) altogether justify the application of Fubini’s theorem, so that (
5.44
) leads to the following expression: for every
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\biggl(\frac{\log(T-t)}{2}+\log 2+\lambda-\frac{\gamma_{\sf EM}}{2}-\int_{y^{\prime\prime}}\int_{y^{\prime}}\phi(y^{\prime\prime})\phi(y^{\prime})\log\lvert y^{\prime\prime}-y^{\prime}\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime\prime}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{r}\int_{y}\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{r}\int_{y}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\times P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r).

By using the previous equality, we can evaluate the limit of
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t)
as
n\to\infty
as follows. The limit of the first term on the right-hand side is straightforward, while the limit of the second term can be obtained by the following calculation:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r}\int_{y}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y,(T+r-t-t^{\prime})^{+}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y,(T+r-t)^{+}\big)\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\times P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{n}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}-\int_{r:T+r/n-t>0}\int_{y}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T+r/n-t}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigl[\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-x^{\prime}-y/n,T+r/n-t-t^{\prime}\big)-\mathfrak{m}_{g}\big(x-y/n,T+r/n-t\big)\bigr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{1}({\mathrm{d}}y,{\mathrm{d}}r)

\displaystyle\quad=-\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-t}[\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-x^{\prime}-y,T-t-t^{\prime})-\mathfrak{m}_{g}(x-y,T-t)]P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})P_{t^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}.

Here, the first equality follows from (
5.46
), (
5.48
) and (
5.51
), before making substitutions for
y
and
r
. The final limit is obtained by using the continuity property in Lemma
5.6
(3
∘
), together with (
5.47
), (
5.50
) and (
5.51
) to justify the application of the dominated convergence theorem. We have shown that
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)
converges to
\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)
for every
(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T)
. Since Proposition
5.4
(3
∘
) already establishes that
\mathfrak{L}\mathbf{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t,T)=[P_{T-t}g(x)]^{2}
, the required limit in (
5.6
) for
\mathfrak{L}f_{n}(x,t,T)
follows.

\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

For any nonnegative
g\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, combining Theorem
2.10
and Theorem
5.2
yields the following equation:

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{L}\mathfrak{m}_{g}^{T}(x,t,T){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)[P_{t}X_{0}(x)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}(x,t)\int_{z_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-t^{\prime}}(x,z_{2})\\
P_{t-t^{\prime}}(x,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}).

(5.53)

A more detailed justification of (
5.53
) is as follows. First, Theorem
2.7
ensures that (
5.53
) holds with
\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}
replaced by the sequence
f_{n}
from Theorem
5.2
. Second, to pass to the limit as
n\to\infty
in the Lebesgue-integral terms using
f_{n}
in place of
\mathfrak{m}^{T}_{g}
, we employ the dominated convergence theorem. This is justified by Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) and the bounds in (
5.6
). By using the limits in (
5.6
), we also determine the limiting values of these Lebesgue-integral terms, given by the left-hand side of (
5.53
) and the first term on its right-hand side. Furthermore, applying Lemma
4.9
with
n=\infty
and the dominated convergence theorem for stochastic integrals shows that (
5.6
) allows the limit in probability for the stochastic integral term with
f_{n}
, thereby recovering the last term in (
5.53
).

To finish the proof of Lemma
5.1
, it remains to rewrite the right-hand side of (
5.53
) so that it matches (
5.1
). This alignment follows directly from the definitions in (
1.15
), (
5.4
), and (
5.5
), along with appropriate substitutions.

\blacksquare

5.2
End of the proof of Theorem
2.11

We start by proving the following proposition on space-time approximations to the identity, which is a rigorous version of (
2.12
).

Proposition 5.7.

Let
\Phi\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be a probability density, and set
\Phi_{\eta}(y)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\eta^{-2}\Phi(\eta^{-1}y)
.
For any
T>0
,
x_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and bounded
\Psi(y,t):{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}
continuous at
(x_{0},0)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}\Psi(x_{0}-y,t){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=\Psi(x_{0},0).

(5.54)

Moreover, if, for some
0<T_{0}<\infty
and
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{0}}\lvert\Psi(y,t)\rvert\leq C(\Psi,T_{0})/(1+\lvert y\rvert^{p})
for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\sup_{T\in(0,T_{0}]}\sup_{\eta\in(0,1/2)}\left|\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}\Psi(x_{0}-y,t){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\right|\leq\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p,\Phi)}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}.

(5.55)

Proof.
To prove (
5.54
), we begin by noting that for any
\delta\in(0,T)
and any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{\delta}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\lvert y\rvert\geq\gamma}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{\lvert y\rvert\geq\gamma}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}P_{t}(y,\eta z)\Phi(z){\mathrm{d}}z\right)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

Therefore, given the continuity of
\Psi
at
(x_{0},0)
, establishing (
5.54
) reduces to verifying the desired limit when
\Psi\equiv 1
. Under this condition, the integrals in (
5.54
) take the following form:

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\iiint_{({\mathbb{R}}^{2})^{3}}P_{t}(y,\eta z_{1})P_{t}(y,\eta z_{2})\Phi(z_{1})\Phi(z_{2}){\mathrm{d}}z_{1}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\iiint_{({\mathbb{R}}^{2})^{3}}P_{t}(\eta y^{\prime},\eta z_{1})P_{t}(\eta y^{\prime},\eta z_{2})\Phi(z_{1})\Phi(z_{2})\eta^{2}{\mathrm{d}}z_{1}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t

(5.56)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{\eta^{-2}T}\iiint_{({\mathbb{R}}^{2})^{3}}P_{t^{\prime}}(y^{\prime},z_{1})P_{t^{\prime}}(y^{\prime},z_{2})\Phi(z_{1})\Phi(z_{2}){\mathrm{d}}z_{1}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}

(5.57)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{\eta^{-2}T}\iint_{({\mathbb{R}}^{2})^{2}}P_{2t^{\prime}}(z_{1},z_{2})\Phi(z_{1})\Phi(z_{2}){\mathrm{d}}z_{1}{\mathrm{d}}z_{2}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime},

(5.58)

where (
5.56
) results from the change of variables
\eta y^{\prime}=y
, (
5.57
) from
t^{\prime}=t/\eta^{2}
, and (
5.58
) by applying the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation. Since
\Phi
has compact support and is a probability density, (
5.58
) implies

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=\lim_{\eta\searrow 0}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\cdot\frac{\log(\eta^{-2}T)}{4\pi}=1.

That is, the required limit with
\Psi\equiv 1
holds. This suffices to prove (
5.54
) in general.

To obtain (
5.55
), recall the assumed decay condition on
\Psi
, and note the bound
1/(1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p})\leq C(p)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{p})/(1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p})
from (
7.8
). Suppose that
\Phi(y)
has support contained in
\lvert y\rvert\leq M
. Then for any
T\in(0,T_{0}]
and
\eta\in(0,1/2)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}\Psi(x_{0}-y,t){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p)(1+\lvert y\rvert^{p})}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p)}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}\biggl(\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t+\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\lvert y\rvert\geq M}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}\lvert y\rvert^{p}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p,\Phi)}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}\biggl(1+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\lvert y\rvert\geq M}\biggl(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}P_{t}(y,z)\Phi_{\eta}(z){\mathrm{d}}z\biggr)^{2}\lvert y\rvert^{p}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p,\Phi)}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}\biggl(1+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}P_{t}(y/2)\lvert y\rvert^{p}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\Psi,T_{0},p,\Phi)}{1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{p}}.

Here, the third inequality bounds
[(2\pi)/(\log\eta^{-1})]\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}[P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(y)]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t
by
C(T,\Phi)
via (
5.54
). The fourth inequality follows from the facts that
\int\Phi_{\eta}(z){\mathrm{d}}z=1
and
P_{t}(y,z)\leq P_{t}(y/2)
for any
z
in
{\rm supp}(\Phi_{\eta})\subseteq\{z^{\prime};\lvert z^{\prime}\rvert\leq M/2\}
and any
\lvert y\rvert\geq M
. The last inequality uses the assumption
p\in(0,\infty)
and the property
{\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{0}[\lvert B_{t}\rvert^{p}]=t^{p/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{0}[\lvert B_{1}\rvert^{p}]
for two-dimensional Brownian motion
\{B_{t}\}
. The final inequality is sufficient to establish (
5.55
).

\blacksquare

Proofs of (
2.29
) and (
2.30
) in Theorem
2.11
.

The proof is organized into seven steps. Steps 1–5 focus on proving
the following result: For all
0<T<\infty
and
f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
&\quad\quad+2\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{x}f(x)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t,\end{split}

(5.59)

where we use the shorthand notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{y}\int_{z_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{(t-s)/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(s-t)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s.

(5.60)

In Step 5, we complete the proof of (
5.59
) and further justify (
2.29
) with
f(x,t)\equiv f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Steps 6 and 7 then provide extensions of
(
2.30
) and (
2.29
) to general
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, respectively, thus completing the proof of Theorem
2.11
.

Step 1.
This proof begins with an application of Lemma
5.1
. We consider the following equation for all
S\in(0,\infty)
, obtained by using (
5.1
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}[P_{S-t}g(x)]^{2}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)=\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}[P_{S-t}g(x)]^{2}\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
&\quad\quad+2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}[P_{S-t}g(x)]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}).\end{split}

(5.61)

To prove (
5.59
), we apply (
5.61
) with the following choice of
g
.
Let
\Phi\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be a fixed probability density. For any
x_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
\eta\in(0,1/2)
, choose

g(y)\equiv\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}}\Phi_{\eta}(y-x_{0}),

where
\Phi_{\eta}(y)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\eta^{-2}\Phi(\eta^{-1}y)
. This function
g
is nonnegative and belongs to
{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, and the function
P_{t}g(x)
admits the following representation:

\displaystyle P_{t}g(x)=\int_{y}P_{t}(x,y+x_{0})\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}}\Phi_{\eta}(y){\mathrm{d}}y=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}}P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0}).

Hence, (
5.61
) applies, and we get the following equation:

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t).

(5.62)

This equation is how our key tool of this proof, Proposition
5.7
, enters.

Step 2.

In this step, we initiate the proof of the following equation: For all
T\in(0,\infty)
and
f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{S=t}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\right){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\left(\int_{S=t}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\right)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl[\int_{x}\left(\int_{S=t}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\right)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.63)

To obtain (
5.63
), first, we integrate both sides of (
5.62
) against
f(x_{0}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S
for
0\leq S\leq T
and
x_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
to get the following equation:

\displaystyle\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S

\displaystyle=\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\int_{t=0}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S.

(5.64)

By Fubini’s theorem, the left-hand side and the first term on the right-hand side correspond to those in (
5.63
). Thus, it remains to show that the final terms in (
5.63
) and the preceding equation are equal. This follows by justifying two applications of the stochastic Fubini theorem according to the following two changes of order of integration:

\displaystyle M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S={\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}SM_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}),\quad{\mathrm{d}}tM_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})=M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.65)

noting that
T\geq S\geq t\geq t^{\prime}\geq 0
in both of those terms of (
5.63
) and (
5.64
).

To justify the two applications of the stochastic Fubini theorem mentioned above, we first note that
\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T]
and
{\mathrm{d}}t
on
[0,T]
are both finite measures. Hence, by Walsh’s
L^{2}
-condition for the stochastic Fubini theorem (see
[
65
, pp.296–297]
), it suffices to check the following two integrability conditions for any
fixed

\eta\in(0,1/2)
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr]<\infty,

(5.66)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl[\int_{x}\left(\int_{S=t}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\right)\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr]<\infty,

(5.67)

which correspond to the first and second equalities in (
5.65
), respectively. In more detail, (
5.66
) and (
5.67
) are relevant because the time variables satisfy
T\geq S\geq t\geq t^{\prime}\geq 0
, matching the structure in the last terms of (
5.63
) and (
5.64
). Steps 3 and 4 below verify (
5.66
) and (
5.67
), respectively. Our main tools are Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
) and the definition of
\mathcal{K}
in (
5.60
).

Step 3.

In this step, we verify (
5.66
) for any fixed
\eta\in(0,1/2)
. To begin, we rewrite its left-hand side. Consider the following portion of (
5.66
), and use (
5.60
) to obtain the first equality below:

\displaystyle\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{y}\int_{z}\begin{bmatrix}P_{(t-s)/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{z}\int_{y}\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}yX_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}s,

(5.68)

where the second uses the rule
\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}=\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{t=s}^{S}
, and

\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)
is a shorthand notation defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{t=s}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\begin{bmatrix}P_{(t-s)/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.69)

As a result of (
5.68
), the expectation in (
5.66
) can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{z}\int_{y}\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)\begin{bmatrix}P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}yX_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}s\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl(\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{\widetilde{z}}\int_{\widetilde{y}}\Psi_{\eta}(S,\widetilde{s},x_{0},\widetilde{y})\begin{bmatrix}P_{\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}}(\widetilde{y},\widetilde{z}_{2})\\
P_{\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}}(\widetilde{y},x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{y}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{z}_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\langle}M_{\infty},{M}_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr].

We proceed to the proof of (
5.66
) by analyzing the right-hand side of the last equality. By integrating out
x^{\prime}
after using (
2.26
) with
p=1/2
, we obtain from the last equality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T)\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{y}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{\widetilde{y}}\frac{\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)\Psi_{\eta}(S,\widetilde{s},x_{0},\widetilde{y})}{(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{y}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S

\displaystyle\quad=C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T)\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{x}\int_{\widetilde{t}=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{t}}\int_{\widetilde{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{1}{(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-\widetilde{t}}\Phi_{\eta}(\widetilde{x}-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widetilde{t}-\widetilde{s})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S,

(5.70)

where the last equality is obtained by writing out
\Psi_{\eta}(S,s,x_{0},y)\Psi_{\eta}(S,\widetilde{s},x_{0},\widetilde{y})
according to the definition (
5.69
) of
\Psi_{\eta}
, integrating out
y
and
\widetilde{y}
, and finally, using
the rule
\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{t=s}^{S}=\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}
. The right-hand side of (
5.70
) can be further simplified to a multiple time integral as follows. First, we consider the bound from the following calculation:

\displaystyle P_{t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{y}P_{t}(y)\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0}-y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\leq C(\Phi,m)\int_{y}P_{t}(y)\biggl(\eta^{-2}\frac{1+\lvert\eta^{-1}x_{0}\rvert^{m}+\lvert\eta^{-1}y\rvert^{m}}{1+\lvert\eta^{-1}x\rvert^{m}}\biggr){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(T,\eta,\Phi,m)(1+\lvert x_{0}\rvert^{m})}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}},\quad\forall\;0<t\leq T,\;m\in\mathbb{N},

(5.71)

where the first inequality holds by (
7.8
). Note that the constant in (
5.71
)
is allowed to depend on
\eta
as we fix
\eta
now.
By (
5.71
) with
m=10
, the bound in (
5.70
) implies the following estimate in terms of a multiple time integral: with
C_{\ref{main3-cond-1-2}}=C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,\eta,\Phi,f)
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\ref{main3-cond-1-2}}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{t}=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{t}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widetilde{t}-\widetilde{s})}{(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}.

(5.72)

To bound the right-hand side of (
5.72
), we show the following two bounds for all
0<t^{\prime}<T
:

\displaystyle\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{t}=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{t}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widetilde{t}-\widetilde{s})}{(s-t^{\prime})^{3/4}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{3/4}}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\beta,T),

(5.73)

\displaystyle\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{t}=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{t}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widetilde{t}-\widetilde{s})}{(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(\beta,T).

(5.74)

To prove (
5.73
), we use (
5.8
). Hence, for
0<T^{\prime}\leq T
and
a\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t){\mathrm{d}}t}{(T^{\prime}-t)^{a}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}/2}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t){\mathrm{d}}t}{(T^{\prime}-t)^{a}}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}/2}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(T^{\prime}-t){\mathrm{d}}t}{t^{a}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\beta,T)}{(T^{\prime}/2)^{a}\log\{[(T^{\prime}/2)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}\}}+\frac{C(\beta,T,a)(T^{\prime}/2)^{1-a}}{[(T^{\prime}/2)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]\log^{2}[(T^{\prime}/2)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\beta,T,a)}{(T^{\prime}/2)^{a}\log\{[(T^{\prime}/2)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}\}}.

(5.75)

In more detail, the first term on the right-hand side of the second inequality comes from integrating
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t)
, while the second term arises from integrating
t^{-a}
. Applying (
5.75
) with
a=3/4
yields the first inequality below:

\displaystyle\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{t}=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{t}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widetilde{t}-\widetilde{s})}{(s-t^{\prime})^{3/4}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{3/4}}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)}{(s-t^{\prime})^{3/4}}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}\leq\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\frac{C(\beta,T){\mathrm{d}}t}{(\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2})^{3/4}\log[(\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2})\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}}\biggr)^{2}\leq C(\beta,T).

This proves (
5.73
). To prove
(
5.74
), it suffices to use
(
5.73
) and the following bound:

\displaystyle\forall\;s>t^{\prime},\;\widetilde{s}>t^{\prime},\quad(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{-1}\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(s-t^{\prime})^{-3/4}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{-3/4}.

This inequality can be obtained as follows:

\displaystyle(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{-1}\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{-1}(s-t^{\prime})^{-1/2}+(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{-1}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(s-t^{\prime})^{-3/4}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{-3/4},

(5.76)

where the last inequality holds because

s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime}=(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{3/4}(s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime})^{1/4}\geq(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{3/4}(s-t^{\prime})^{1/4},

and similarly,
s+\widetilde{s}-2t^{\prime}\geq(s-t^{\prime})^{3/4}(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{1/4}
. We have proved both (
5.73
) and (
5.74
).

We complete the verification of (
5.66
) now. By using (
5.72
) and (
5.74
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{S=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}\lvert f(x_{0})\rvert\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{S}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{S}\int_{x}\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\biggr]\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,\eta,\Phi,f)<\infty.

The verification of (
5.66
) for any fixed
\eta\in(0,1/2)
is complete.

Step 4.

To verify (
5.67
), we begin with the following inequality, which is implied by Proposition
5.7
with
\Psi(y,t)\equiv f(y)
: for any
\eta\in(0,1/2)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl[\int_{x}\left(\int_{S=t}^{T}\int_{x_{0}}f(x_{0})\frac{2\pi}{\log\eta^{-1}}[P_{S-t}\Phi_{\eta}(x-x_{0})]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}{\mathrm{d}}S\right)\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{x}\frac{C(f,T,\Phi,m)}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr)^{2}{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr].

(5.77)

Note that the constant
C(f,T,\Phi,m)
used here is independent of
\eta
. Because of this, (
5.77
) will be applied again in Step 5.

From the definition (
5.60
) of
\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and the moment bound in (
2.26
) with
p=1/2
, it follows that for all integers
m\geq 10
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{x}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr)^{2}{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T)\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{x}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{y}\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{y}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{(t-s)/2}(x,y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,x^{\prime})\times\frac{1}{1+\lvert\widetilde{x}\rvert^{m}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{(t-\widetilde{s})/2}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{y})\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-\widetilde{s})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}P_{\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}}(\widetilde{y},x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{y}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t}{\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,m)\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-\widetilde{s}){\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t}{{\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}}<\infty.

(5.78)

The two inequalities in (
5.78
) are obtained by using the following consideration.
To get the first inequality, we integrate out
\widetilde{y},\widetilde{x},y,x,x^{\prime}
; this uses in particular the following identity:

\int_{\widetilde{x}}\int_{\widetilde{y}}\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{y}P_{(t-s)/2}(x,y)P_{s-t^{\prime}}(y,x^{\prime})P_{\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}}(\widetilde{y},x^{\prime})P_{(t-\widetilde{s})/2}(\widetilde{x},\widetilde{y}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{y}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}=1,\quad\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

Also, to justify the last inequality in (
5.78
), we have used (
5.79
) which we now derive:

\displaystyle\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{t}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s){\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-\widetilde{s})}{\min\{s-t^{\prime},\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime}\}^{1/2}}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}

\displaystyle\quad=2\int_{s=t^{\prime}}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-s)\int_{\widetilde{s}=t^{\prime}}^{s}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t-\widetilde{s})}{(\widetilde{s}-t^{\prime})^{1/2}}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{s}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\quad=2\int_{\widecheck{s}=0}^{t-t^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widecheck{s})\int_{\widehat{s}=t-s}^{t-t^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\widehat{s})}{(t-t^{\prime}-\widehat{s})^{1/2}}{\mathrm{d}}\widehat{s}{\mathrm{d}}\widecheck{s}\quad\mbox{($\because\widehat{s}=t-\widetilde{s},\;\widecheck{s}=t-s$)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(\beta,T)}{[(t^{\prime}-t)\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{1/2}\log^{2}[(t-t^{\prime})\wedge\frac{1}{2}]^{-1}},

(5.79)

where the last inequality follows by using (
5.75
) with
T^{\prime}=t-t^{\prime}
and
a=1/2
, as well as (
5.8
). By (
5.77
) and (
5.78
), we have verified (
5.67
).

Step 5.
The final step in the proof of (
5.59
) is to take the limits of both sides of (
5.63
) as
\eta\searrow 0
. Note that by Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
),

\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{x^{\prime}}(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{10})^{-1}\langle M_{\infty},M_{\infty}\rangle({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})<\infty
almost surely. Therefore, by dominated convergence, Proposition
5.7
implies that as
\eta\searrow 0
, the left-hand side of (
5.63
) converges almost surely to the left-hand side of (
5.59
). The same proposition ensures that the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.63
) converges almost surely to the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.59
). Finally, by using Proposition
5.7
and the bound in (
5.78
), together with Itô’s isometry, the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.63
) converges in
L^{2}({\mathbb{P}})
to the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.59
). This establishes (
5.59
), thus (
2.30
) with
f(x,t)\equiv f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
.

To establish (
2.29
) for
f(x,t)\equiv f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, we apply Walsh’s
L^{2}
-condition from the stochastic Fubini theorem
[
65
, pp.296–297]
, with justification provided by (
5.78
).

Step 6.
In this step, we prove (
2.30
) using a standard approximation argument based on (
5.59
) and (
5.78
). Given
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, we begin with the following sequence of functions, which converges uniformly to
f
:

f_{N}(x,t)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,f(x,0)\mathds{1}_{\{0\}}(t)+\sum_{n=1}^{2^{N}}f\left(x,\frac{nT}{2^{N}}\right)\mathds{1}_{\left(\frac{(n-1)T}{2^{N}},\frac{nT}{2^{N}}\right]}(t),\quad N\in\mathbb{N}.

Then, by linearity and (
5.59
), equation (
2.30
) holds with
f
replaced by
f_{N}
.

Our goal in this step is to show that, when
f
is replaced by
f_{N}
, the term on the left-hand side of (
2.30
) and the two terms on the right-hand side of (
2.29
) all admit taking the limit as
N\to\infty
under the integral signs. Specifically, we show below that the three limits
in (
5.80
), (
5.81
) and (
5.83
) below, hold:

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f_{N}(x,t){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{\rm a.s.}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t),

(5.80)

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f_{N}(x,t)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{\rm a.s.}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X^{\otimes 2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(5.81)

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{x}f_{N}(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f_{N}(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})

(5.82)

\displaystyle\quad\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{L^{2}({\mathbb{P}})}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}f(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})

(5.83)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{x}f(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,s,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t.

(5.84)

Here, the equalities in (
5.82
) and (
5.84
) follow from applying the stochastic Fubini theorem. This application is justified, as (
5.78
) suffices for the relevant version of Walsh’s
L^{2}
-condition
[
65
, pp.296–297]
.

The limits in (
5.80
), (
5.81
), and (
5.83
) can be justified as follows. Both (
5.80
) and (
5.81
) follow from the dominated convergence theorem. In particular, the verification of (
5.80
) relies on the almost sure bound
\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{x^{\prime}}(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{10})^{-1}\langle M,M\rangle({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})<\infty
, as noted in Step 5. To prove (
5.83
), it suffices to observe that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\left(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}(f-f_{N})(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,t,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}s\right){M}_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})\biggr)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{N\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\left(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}(f-f_{N})(x,t)\mathcal{K}(x,s,z_{1},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\right)^{2}{\langle}{M}_{\infty},{M}_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})\biggr]=0.

Here, the last equality holds by dominated convergence, because for all integers
m\geq 10
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{x^{\prime}}\left(\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{x}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\right)^{2}{\langle}{M}_{\infty},{M}_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{s}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{x}\frac{1}{1+\lvert x\rvert^{m}}\mathcal{K}(x,t,x^{\prime},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\biggr)^{2}T{\langle}{M}_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr]<\infty,

where the first inequality follows by applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to the
{\mathrm{d}}s
-integral on its left-hand side, and the last inequality follows from (
5.78
).

By (
5.80
), (
5.81
), and (
5.83
), we have extended (
5.59
) such that for all
f(x,t)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times[0,T])
, (
2.30
) holds almost surely for all
0<T<\infty
.

Step 7.
The proof of (
2.29
) has already been provided in Step 6, since the limit in (
5.83
) directly enables an extension by invoking (
2.29
) with
f(x,t)\equiv f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. The proof of Theorem
2.11
is now complete.

\blacksquare

Proof of (
2.31
) of Theorem
2.11
.

We consider the second term on the right-hand side of (
2.29
) when
f(x,t)\equiv f(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{pd}^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. This term can be written explicitly as follows:

\displaystyle 2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{t=t^{\prime}}^{T}\int_{z_{2}}\int_{x}f(x)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t-t^{\prime}}(x;z_{1},z_{2})X({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}tM({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{t=0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\int_{z_{2}}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s/2}f(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-s}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-s}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}sX_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}tM_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=2\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T}\int_{z_{1}}\int_{s=0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s/2}f(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{z_{2}}\int_{t=s}^{T-t^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-s}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-s}(y,z_{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}tX_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}sM_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}).

(5.85)

Here, the third equality follows from interchanging the order of integration.

To establish (
2.31
), it suffices to verify Walsh’s
L^{2}
-condition for the stochastic Fubini theorem
[
65
, pp.296–297]
, which, in this case, reduces to the following condition:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s/2}f(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-s}\int_{x^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{z_{2}}\int_{t=s}^{T-t^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-s}(y,z_{2})\\
P_{t-s}(y,x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}tX_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},t^{\prime})\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl(\int_{\widetilde{z}_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{t}=s}^{T-t^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\widetilde{t}-s}(y,\widetilde{z}_{2})\\
P_{\widetilde{t}-s}(y,x^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{z}_{2},t^{\prime})\biggr){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s<\infty

(5.86)

To bound the left-hand side, we first apply (
2.26
), integrating out
z_{2}
and
\widetilde{z}_{2}
, and then integrate out
x^{\prime}
. Thus, for any
p\in(0,1)
, up to a multiplicative constant depending only on
(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)
, the left-hand side of (
5.86
) is bounded by the following integral:

\displaystyle\int_{s=0}^{T}\int_{y}\begin{bmatrix}P_{s/2}f(y)\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{t^{\prime}=0}^{T-s}\int_{t=s}^{T-t^{\prime}}\int_{\widetilde{t}=s}^{T-t^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\min\{t-s,\widetilde{t}-s\}^{p}}\frac{1}{t+\widetilde{t}-2s}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s.

This integral is finite because

\displaystyle\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{t}=0}^{T}\frac{1}{\min\{t,\widetilde{t}\}^{p}(t+\widetilde{t})}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=2\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{\widetilde{t}=0}^{t}\frac{1}{(\widetilde{t})^{p}(t+\widetilde{t})}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{t}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq C(p)\int_{t=0}^{T}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}t}{t^{p}}<\infty;

see Lemma
6.15
for justification of the penultimate inequality. Therefore, (
5.86
) is satisfied, allowing us to interchange
{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s
and
M_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},{\mathrm{d}}t^{\prime})
in (
5.85
) to obtain (
2.31
).

\blacksquare

6
Moments and their applications

In this section, we consider solutions
X_{\varepsilon}
to the approximate SHE in (
1.4
), where the initial conditions
X_{0}(\cdot)
are bounded, nonnegative, and independent of
{\varepsilon}
. Our aim is to prove Theorem
3.4
, Theorem
3.5
, and Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
). The proofs appear in Sections
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.3
, respectively.

6.1
Proof of Theorem
3.4
: approximate mixed moment bounds

We first provide preliminary results and definitions in Sections
6.1.1
and
6.1.2
, following the methodology of
[
18
]
. The proof of Theorem
3.4
is then presented in Section
6.1.3
. Note that our notation below differs slightly from that of
[
18
]
.

6.1.1
Diagrammatic expansions

In the sequel, we work with the following generalization of (
2.1
). For any
{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]
, integer
N\geq 1
, and
x_{0}=(x_{0}^{1},\cdots,x_{0}^{N})
with
x_{0}^{k}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
for all
1\leq k\leq N
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{N}X_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{i},t)\Bigg]={\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{x_{0}}\Bigg[\exp\Bigg\{\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}r\varphi_{\varepsilon}(B^{\mathbf{i}}_{r})\Bigg\}\prod_{i=1}^{N}X_{0}(B_{t}^{i})\Bigg].\end{split}

(6.1)

Here, we use the following notations:
\mathcal{E}_{N}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\{(i\prime,i)\in\mathbb{N}^{2};1\leq i<i\prime\leq N\}
;
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}
is chosen to satisfy (
1.5
); with
\phi_{\varepsilon}
defined in (
3.17
),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)

\displaystyle\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\phi_{\varepsilon}({\sqrt{2}}x)={\varepsilon}^{-2}\varphi({\varepsilon}^{-1}x)\quad(\varphi\equiv\varphi_{1});

(6.2)

the processes
B^{1},\cdots,B^{N}
are independent two-dimensional standard Brownian motions collectively written as
B=(B^{1},\cdots,B^{N})
with
B^{k}_{0}=x^{k}_{0}
under
{\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{x_{0}}
so that

\displaystyle B^{\mathbf{i}}_{t}\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{B^{i\prime}_{t}-B_{t}^{i}}{{\sqrt{2}}},\quad\mathbf{i}=(i\prime,i)\in\mathcal{E}_{N},

(6.3)

define two-dimensional standard Brownian motions. We also assume that
B
is independent of
X_{\varepsilon}
.

The proof of Theorem
3.4
relies crucially on the series expansion in the proposition below, where the Dirac delta functions are used rigorously through the following definition: for any
a\in I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
and function
F
defined on
I
,

\int_{I}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\delta_{a}(\tau)F(\tau)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,F(a).

Proposition 6.1.

For any given nonnegative function
f
defined on
({\mathbb{R}}^{2})^{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{x_{0}}\Bigg[\exp\Bigg\{\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}}r\varphi_{\varepsilon}(B^{\mathbf{i}}_{r})\Bigg\}f(B_{t})\Bigg]\\
&\quad={\mathbb{E}}^{B}_{x_{0}}[f(B_{t})]+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}}}{{\mathbf{i}_{1}\neq\cdots\neq\mathbf{i}_{m}}}}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\int_{0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t}{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{s}_{m}Q^{\lambda,\phi;\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},t}f(x_{0}),\end{split}

(6.4)

where

\boldsymbol{s}_{m}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(s_{1},\cdots,s_{m})
,
Q^{\lambda,\phi;\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},t}f(x_{0})
are iterated integrals
defined as follows:

\displaystyle Q^{\lambda,\phi;\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},t}f(x_{0})

\displaystyle\quad{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{[s_{1},s_{2})}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}\cdots\int_{[s_{m},t)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{m}\Bigg(\prod_{\scriptstyle\ell:\sigma_{\ell}=0\atop\scriptstyle 1\leq\ell\leq m}\delta_{s_{\ell}}(\tau_{\ell})\Bigg)\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};0}\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};1}\cdots\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};m}f,

(6.5)

and
\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};\ell}F
, given by iterated Lebesgue integrals depending on
(\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{m},s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},t)
, are the
graphical integrals
defined in
[
18
]
.

For
N\geq 3
, Proposition
6.1
follows by combining
[
18
, (2.12), (4.1), and (4.43)]
. For
N=2
, the triple series in (
6.4
) simplifies to two terms with
m=1
. While
[
18
]
does not directly connect this case to graphical integrals, (
6.4
) can be derived more simply using
[
18
, (2.39) and Lemma 4.4]
, as shown in Lemma
6.10
.

While implicit due to the graphical integrals, the key feature of the expansion in (
6.4
) is that, at any given time, no more than one pair of particles interacts through the “binding” of
B^{i\prime}
and
B^{i}
with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}
. This property arises from the following elementary integral expansion: for all
t\in(0,\infty)
, finite sets
\mathcal{E}
such that
E=\#\mathcal{E}\geq 1
, and Borel measurable functions
\varphi(\mathbf{i},r):\mathcal{E}\times[0,t]\to{\mathbb{R}}
either nonnegative or bounded, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\exp\Bigg\{\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(\mathbf{i},r){\mathrm{d}}r\Bigg\}\\
&\quad=1+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptstyle\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m}\in\mathcal{E}}}{{\mathbf{i}_{1}\neq\cdots\neq\mathbf{i}_{m}}}}\int_{0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t}\prod_{n=1}^{m}\varphi(\mathbf{i}_{n},s_{n})\exp\Bigg\{\int_{s_{n}}^{s_{n+1}}\varphi(\mathbf{i}_{n},r){\mathrm{d}}r\Bigg\}{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{s}_{m}.\end{split}

(6.6)

Here, under the integral over
0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t
, we write
s_{m+1}=t
and
\boldsymbol{s}_{m}=(s_{1},\cdots,s_{m})
. The original proof of (
6.6
) for
N\geq 3
in
[
18
]
is based on Poisson random measures. This approach is motivated by the fact that Poisson point processes in
\mathcal{E}
exhibit nonconcurrent (
0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t
) and nonconsecutive (
\mathbf{i}_{1}\neq\cdots\neq\mathbf{i}_{m}
) arrivals, as seen in an earlier resolvent expansion in
[
41
]
when establishing limiting moments for the case that
X_{0}(\cdot)
is in
L^{2}
. However, (
6.6
) can also be derived more directly by starting with the following argument and iterating indefinitely when
\varphi(\mathbf{i},r)
is bounded: by applying the fundamental theorem of calculus to the functions
s\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{s,t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\exp\{\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\mathbf{j}\in\mathcal{E}}\varphi(\mathbf{j},r){\mathrm{d}}r\}
and
s\mapsto\exp\{\sum_{\mathbf{j}:\mathbf{j}\neq\mathbf{i}}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi(\mathbf{j},r){\mathrm{d}}r\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0,t}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{\mathbf{i}_{1}\in\mathcal{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s_{1},\mathbf{i}_{1})\mathcal{E}_{s_{1},t}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{\mathbf{i}_{1}\in\mathcal{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s_{1},\mathbf{i}_{1})\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{i}_{1}}_{s_{1},t}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}+\sum_{\mathbf{i}_{1}\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\mathbf{i}_{2}:\mathbf{i}_{2}\neq\mathbf{i}_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s_{1},\mathbf{i}_{1})\int_{s_{1}}^{t}\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{i}_{1}}_{s_{1},s_{2}}\varphi(s_{2},\mathbf{i}_{2})\mathcal{E}_{s_{2},t}{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1},

where
\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{i}}_{s,t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\exp\{\int_{s}^{t}\varphi(\mathbf{i},r){\mathrm{d}}r\}
.

To facilitate the forthcoming applications of Proposition
6.1
,
we recall the definitions of the graphical integrals that validate (
6.5
) in Section
6.1.2
and incorporate some minor extensions as well. Also, we often adopt the following change of variables:

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{1}&=s_{1},\;u_{2}=s_{2}-\tau_{1},\cdots,\;u_{m}=s_{m}-\tau_{m-1},\;u_{m+1}=t-\tau_{m},\\
v_{1}&=\tau_{1}-s_{1},\;v_{2}=\tau_{2}-s_{2},\cdots,v_{m}=\tau_{m}-s_{m}.\end{split}

(6.7)

This and (
6.5
) yield the following alternative representations of the summands in (
6.4
):

\displaystyle\int_{0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t}{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{s}_{m}Q^{\lambda,\phi;\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m};{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},t}f(x_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}<t}{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{s}_{m}\int_{[s_{1},s_{2})}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{1}\cdots\int_{[s_{m},t)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau_{m}\Bigg(\prod_{\scriptstyle\ell:\sigma_{\ell}=0\atop\scriptstyle 1\leq\ell\leq m}\delta_{s_{\ell}}(\tau_{\ell})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};0}\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};1}\cdots\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};m}f

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\Delta_{m}(t)}{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\otimes{\mathrm{d}}\boldsymbol{v}_{m}\Bigg(\prod_{\scriptstyle\ell:\sigma_{\ell}=0\atop\scriptstyle 1\leq\ell\leq m}\delta_{0}(v_{\ell})\Bigg)\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};0}\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};1}\cdots\int{\mathrm{d}}\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{{\varepsilon};m}f,

(6.8)

where
\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(u_{1},\cdots,u_{m})
,
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(v_{1},\cdots,v_{m})
, and

\displaystyle\Delta_{m}(t)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\Biggl\{(\boldsymbol{u}_{m},\boldsymbol{v}_{m})\in(0,t)^{m}\times[0,t)^{m};\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}(u_{\ell}+v_{\ell})<t\Biggr\}.

(6.9)

6.1.2
Definition of the graphical integrals

Throughout Section
6.1.2
, we fix
{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{i}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{i}_{m}\in\mathcal{E}_{N}
with
\mathbf{i}_{1}\neq\cdots\neq\mathbf{i}_{m}
, and
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.
Also,
s_{1},\cdots,s_{m},x_{0}
satisfy
0<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{m}=t
and
x_{0}=(x_{0}^{1},\cdots,x_{0}^{N})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2N}
. With
m
held fixed, it will be convenient to use the following notations as well:

\displaystyle s_{0}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,0,\quad s_{m+1}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,t,\quad\mathbf{i}_{m+1}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\varnothing.

(6.10)

The graphical integrals in (
6.5
) are defined in Step 1–3 below. Roughly speaking, these steps define the following objects: (1) weighted graphs
\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\varepsilon}
in space and time; (2) subgraphs
\{\mathcal{G}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};0},\ldots,\mathcal{G}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\varepsilon};m}\}
decomposing
\mathcal{G}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\varepsilon}\setminus\{(x_{0}^{k},s_{0})\}_{k=1}^{N}
; and (3) the graphical integrals according to the subgraphs. Figure
2
gives an example illustrating the definitions in the first two steps.
