\displaystyle\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}\phi(\overline{z})P_{2s_{1}}({\varepsilon}x_{0}^{0}-{\varepsilon}\overline{z})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}\phi(\overline{z})\int_{0}^{{\varepsilon}^{-2}\lvert x_{0}^{0}-\overline{z}\rvert^{-2}T}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}u}{4\pi u}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4u}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda,T)\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\overline{z}\phi(\overline{z})\lvert\log\lvert x_{0}^{0}-\overline{z}\rvert\rvert+1\right),

(6.94)

where the last inequality uses the definition (
1.5
) of
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}
.

\blacksquare

6.2
Proof of Theorem
3.5
: joint tightness

Recall the definitions of
X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
,
\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
,
\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
, and
\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
in (
3.34
), and observe the following properties:

(P1)

By
[
35
, 2.4 Proposition, p.107]
, the family of laws of
\{(X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon},\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon},\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon},\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}
is tight as probability measures on the product Polish space
C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)\times C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}[0,\infty)\times C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})}[0,\infty)\times C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})}[0,\infty)
if and only if the marginals are tight. Here,
\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is Polish for each
d\in\mathbb{N}
, as established by normalizing measures and applying
[
35
, 1.7 Theorem, p.101]
, and
marginal tightness
means that the family of laws from each set below is tight as probability measures on
C_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{r_{j}})}[0,\infty)
, with
r_{1}=r_{2}=2
for the first two sets and
r_{3}=r_{4}=4
for the last two sets:

\displaystyle\{X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]},\;\{\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]},\;\{\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]},\;\{\mathring{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}.

(6.95)

(P2)

The set
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, consisting of functions on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with bounded continuous partial derivatives up to second order, forms a separating class in
{\mathscr{C}}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and includes the constant function
1
. A separating class is a collection of functions such that if two finite measures agree on all functions in the class, the measures are identical (see the definition preceding
[
58
, Theorem II.4.1]
).

By (P1), it suffices to establish marginal tightness. Let
D_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{r_{j}})}[0,\infty)
denote the space of
\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{r_{j}})
-valued càdlàg functions on
[0,\infty)
with Skorokhod’s
J_{1}
-topology. Because all processes in (
6.95
) are continuous,
[
35
, Problem 25, p.153]
further reduces this to verifying that, for each set, the corresponding family of laws is tight as probability measures on
D_{\mathcal{M}_{f}({\mathbb{R}}^{r_{j}})}[0,\infty)
and that the family of laws is
C
-tight: every subsequential limit law corresponds to an almost surely continuous process. To establish this, we apply
[
58
, Theorem II.4.1]
with
(E,D_{0})
taken as
({\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))
or
({\mathbb{R}}^{4},{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{4}))
, as justified by (P2). It remains to verify conditions (C1)–(C4) below:

(C1)

For any
\eta>0
and
0<T<\infty
, there exist
0\leq f_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and
K\in(0,\infty)
with
f_{0}(x)=1
for all
\lvert x\rvert>K
such that

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{P}}\Biggl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\rho_{\varepsilon}(f_{0},t)>\eta\Biggr)<\eta,\quad\forall\;\rho\in\{X^{\sharp},\nu^{\sharp}\}.

(C2)

For any
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and
\rho\in\{X^{\sharp},\nu^{\sharp}\}
, the family of laws of
\{\rho_{\varepsilon}(f,t)\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}
is
C
-tight as probability measures on
D_{\mathbb{R}}[0,\infty)
.

(C3)

For any
\eta>0
and
0<T<\infty
, there exist
0\leq g_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})
and
L\in(0,\infty)
with
g_{0}(x,y)=1
for all
\lvert(x,y)\rvert>L
,
(x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
, such that

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{P}}\Biggl(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\rho_{\varepsilon}(g_{0},t)>\eta\Biggr)<\eta,\quad\forall\;\rho\in\{\mu^{\sharp},\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}\}.

(C4)

For any
g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})
and
\rho\in\{\mu^{\sharp},\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}\}
, the family of laws of
\{\rho_{\varepsilon}(g,t)\}_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}
is
C
-tight as probability measures on
D_{\mathbb{R}}[0,\infty)
.

The verifications of (C1) and (C2) are provided in Steps 1 and 2 below. Because the definitions of
\nu_{\varepsilon}
,
\mu_{\varepsilon}
, and
\mathring{\mu}_{\varepsilon}
differ only slightly, we address (C3) and (C4) in Step 3 by highlighting the key inequalities; the remaining arguments closely follow those for (C1) and (C2) with
\rho\equiv\nu^{\sharp}
.

Step 1.
To verify (C1), we consider the case of
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
first. The key bound for this purpose is given by the second inequality below, while the first inequality is deduced from the weak formulation in (
2.21
): for any
0\leq f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f,t)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq X_{\varepsilon}(f\psi,0)+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{t=0}^{T}X_{\varepsilon}\left(\left|\frac{\Delta(f\psi)}{2}\right|,t\right){\mathrm{d}}t\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\lvert M_{\varepsilon}(f\psi,t)\rvert\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{x}(f\psi)(x)X_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{t=0}^{T}X_{\varepsilon}\left(\left|\frac{\Delta(f\psi)}{2}\right|,t\right){\mathrm{d}}t\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x_{0}^{1}}\int_{x_{0}^{0}}(f\psi)(x_{0}^{1})(f\psi)(x_{0}^{1}+{\varepsilon}x_{0}^{0})\phi(x_{0}^{0})\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{1},t)X_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{1}+{\varepsilon}x_{0}^{0},t){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr]^{1/2}.

In more detail,
\psi(x)\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,(1+\lvert x\rvert^{10})^{-1}
by the definition of
X^{\sharp}
in (
3.34a
), and the last term follows by applying the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
61
, (4.1) Theorem, p.160]
, (
3.3b
), and Jensen’s inequality. For the right-hand side of the last inequality, we can bound the first term by using the assumption that
X_{0}(\cdot)
is bounded, the second term by using the mild form (
2.18
), and the third term by using Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
). Hence, by dominated convergence, for any
\eta>0
, there exists
(f_{0},K)
such that
0\leq f_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and
K\in(0,\infty)
with
f_{0}(x)=1
for all
\lvert x\rvert>K
, and

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f_{0},t)\biggr]<\eta^{2}.

By Markov’s inequality, the foregoing inequality is enough to verify (C1) for
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
.

The verification of (C1) for
\rho\equiv\nu^{\sharp}
is similar. The main difference is that we proceed with the following bound now instead of the key bound mentioned above for
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
:

\displaystyle\forall\;0\leq f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\quad\;{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f,t)\biggr]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}(f\psi)(x)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr],

(6.96)

which follows immediately from the definition of
\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
in (
3.34a
). By Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
), the preceding argument verifying (C1) for
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
also applies here, yielding the required condition in (C1) with
\rho\equiv\nu^{\sharp}
. This completes the verification of (C1).

Step 2.
We verify (C2) in this step by showing that the processes under consideration satisfy
Kolmogorov’s criterion for weak compactness
[
61
, (1.8) Theorem, pp.517–518]
.

For
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
, we work with
\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}
as the solution to (
1.4
) when the initial condition is the constant function
\mathds{1}(\cdot)
. Then by the weak formulation (
2.21
) and the inequality
(a+b)^{4}\lesssim a^{4}+b^{4}
for all
a,b\geq 0
, we get, for any
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert X_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}(f,T_{2})-X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f,T_{1})\rvert^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}X_{\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\Delta(f\psi)}{2},t\right){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr|^{4}\biggr]+{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}(f\psi)(x){M}_{\varepsilon}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)\biggr|^{4}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty}){\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}\left(\biggl|\frac{\Delta(f\psi)}{2}\biggr|,T\right)^{4}\biggr](T_{2}-T_{1})^{4}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{0}^{1}}\int_{x_{0}^{0}}(f\psi)(x_{0}^{1})(f\psi)(x_{0}^{1}+{\varepsilon}x_{0}^{0})\phi(x_{0}^{0})\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{1},t)X_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{1}+{\varepsilon}x_{0}^{0},t){\mathrm{d}}x_{0}^{0}{\mathrm{d}}x_{0}^{1}{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}\biggr].

Here, the first term on the right-hand side follows by applying to the first term on the left-hand side Jensen’s inequality and then the increasing monotonicity of
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}[\prod_{j=1}^{4}\widetilde{X}_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}^{j},t)]
for all
x_{0}^{1},x_{0}^{2},x_{0}^{3},x_{0}^{4}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, which can be seen by using (
6.1
), and the last term in the foregoing inequality follows by using the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
61
, (4.1) Theorem, p.160]
and (
3.3b
). To use the last inequality, note that
the expectation in the first term admits a bound uniformly in
{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]
, which is a particular consequence of
[
18
, Theorem 2.3]
, and
we can bound the last term by Theorem
3.4
(1
∘
) with
p=1/2
. Hence,

\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert X_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}(f,T_{2})-X^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f,T_{1})\rvert^{4}\right]\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,f)(T_{2}-T_{1})^{3/2},\;\forall\;0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T.

We have verified (C2) for
\rho\equiv X^{\sharp}
since Kolmogorov’s criterion for weak compactness applies.

To verify (C2) for
\rho\equiv\nu^{\sharp}
, Theorem
3.4
(1
∘
) with
p=1/2
is applied again to get the second inequality below:

\displaystyle\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}(f,T_{2})-\nu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(f,T_{1})\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{{\varepsilon}\in(0,{{\varepsilon}_{\ref{def:ovl}}(\lambda)}]}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}(f\psi)(x)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)^{2}\biggr]

(6.97)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,f)(T_{2}-T_{1})^{3/2},\;\forall\;0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T.

The required
C
-tightness now follows by using Kolmogorov’s criterion for weak compactness.

Step 3.
To verify (C3) and (C4), note that the definitions of
\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
and
\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
in (
3.34b
) yield the following bounds, where
0\leq g\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{4})
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(g,t)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\;\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g({\varepsilon}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}y+x)\psi(x)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y+x,t)X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\right],

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(g,t)\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\;\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}\int_{y}g(x,y)\psi(x)\psi(y)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y+x,t)X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr],

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\lvert\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}(g,T_{2})-\mu^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(g,T_{1})\rvert^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\;\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\left(\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{y}g({\varepsilon}y+x,x)\psi({\varepsilon}y+x)\psi(x)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y+x,t)X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\right)^{2}\biggr],

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\lvert\mathring{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}(g,T_{2})-\mathring{\mu}^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(g,T_{1})\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\;\leq{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\left(\int_{t=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x}\int_{y}g(x,y)\psi(x)\psi(y)\Lambda_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon}({\varepsilon}y+x,t)X_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\right)^{2}\biggr].

Here, the first two inequalities are comparable to (
6.96
), and the last two inequalities are comparable to (
6.97
). We omit the remaining details for the reason mentioned before Step 1.

6.3
Proof of Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
): covariation identity and bounds

Proof of (
2.24
) in Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
).

We begin by proving the following identity:

\displaystyle\forall\;h\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}),\quad{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x){\mathbb{E}}[\Lambda_{n}X_{n}(x,t)^{2}]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

(6.98)

To justify (
6.98
), we use the following direct consequence of Corollary
3.8
:

\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x)\nu_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x)\nu_{n}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}t)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x)\Lambda_{n}X_{n}(x,t)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.

Theorem
3.4
(1
∘
) ensures that the sequence of random variables in the rightmost limit is
L^{2}({\mathbb{P}})
-bounded, and thus forms a uniformly integrable family. Consequently, by a standard result on uniform integrability (see
[
6
, 6.5.2 Theorem, p.263]
), (
6.98
) follows.

The rest of this proof is devoted to justifying the following identity:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x){\mathbb{E}}[\Lambda_{n}X_{n}(x,t)^{2}]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{t=0}^{T}\int_{x}h(x)\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X_{0}^{\otimes 2}(x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t,

(6.99)

which we address through several steps. Step 1 demonstrates that the identity follows from the right-hand side of (
6.98
). This step adapts the method used in
[
18
, Proposition 2.6]
and assumes that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)F_{x,T_{0}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)P_{t-\tau}F_{x,T_{0}}(0){\mathrm{d}}\tau,\quad\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;0\leq t<\infty,\;0<T_{0}<\infty.

(6.100)

Here, we use the notation below (
6.1
) with
\mathbf{i}_{0}=(2,1)
, and define

\displaystyle B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{t}\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\frac{B^{2}_{t}+B^{1}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}},\quad F_{x,T_{0}}(y)\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\;{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}}_{{\sqrt{2}}x}\biggl[X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{T_{0}}+y}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{T_{0}}-y}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)\biggr].

Step 2 turns to the proof of (
6.100
), where we modify the argument from
[
18
, Proof of (5.13) for Proposition 5.2, p.183]
.

Step 1.
To prove (
6.99
), we first utilize the moment duality in (
2.1
). Hence, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{n}(x,t)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(B^{1},B^{2})}_{(x,x)}\biggl[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\prod_{i=1}^{2}X_{0}(B^{i}_{t})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{{\varepsilon}}{\mathbb{E}}^{(B^{1},B^{2})}_{(x,x)}\biggl[\exp\biggl(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\biggr)X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{t}+B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{t}-B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t}}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)\biggr].

Observe that
B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}
and
B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}
are independent two-dimensional standard Brownian motions. By integrating out
B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{n}(x,t)^{2}]=\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)F_{x,t}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right].

Then by (
6.100
) with
T_{0}=t
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{n}(x,t)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}[F_{x,t}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-\tau})]{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}}_{{\sqrt{2}}x}\biggl[{\mathbb{E}}^{(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}},B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime})}_{(0,B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{\tau})}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{t-\tau}+B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-\tau}}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)X_{0}\biggl(\frac{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{t-\tau}-B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-\tau}}{{\sqrt{2}}}\biggr)\biggr]\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}}_{{\sqrt{2}}x}\biggl[{\mathbb{E}}^{(B^{1},B^{2})}_{(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{\tau}/{\sqrt{2}},B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}\prime}_{\tau}/{\sqrt{2}})}{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{2}X_{0}(B^{i}_{t-\tau})\biggr]\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\int_{z^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{\tau/2}(x,z^{\prime})\\
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}\begin{bmatrix}P_{t-\tau}(z^{\prime},x_{2}^{2})\\
P_{t-\tau}(z^{\prime},x_{2}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}X_{0}(x_{2}^{2})\\
X_{0}(x_{2}^{1})\end{bmatrix}_{\times}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}^{2}\\
{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}^{1}\end{bmatrix}_{\otimes}{\mathrm{d}}z^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}X_{0}^{\otimes 2}(x),

(6.101)

where
\mathring{{\rm DBG}}\mbox{}^{\beta}_{t}F(x)
is as defined in (
1.15
). Moreover, by Theorem
3.4
(3
∘
), we can apply (
6.101
) to obtain (
6.99
) via dominated convergence, since
h(x)\in{\mathscr{C}}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
by assumption.

Step 2.
In this step, we prove (
6.100
), which will be accomplished in Step 2-3 below after demonstrating two limits in Steps 2-1 and 2-2.

Step 2-1.
The first limit is as follows:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y}\phi(y){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t=0.

(6.102)

To prove (
6.102
), we use the expansion
{\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{0}^{t}h(s){\mathrm{d}}s}=1+\int_{0}^{t}h(s){\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{s}^{t}h(r){\mathrm{d}}r}{\mathrm{d}}s
. Hence, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}x}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}x,{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{3}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{s}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}x,{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t-s}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t.

(6.103)

We use this identity for
x=0
and
x=y/{\sqrt{2}}
, integrate out
y
against
\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y
, and subtract the resulting equations. We claim that in the limit, the two resulting terms on the right-hand side vanish. For the first term, we perform the following calculation, utilizing the fact that
\phi
is a probability density, as stated in
Proposition
3.1
(1
∘
):

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{t}(0,{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-q{{\varepsilon}}_{n}^{2}t}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}\lvert P_{t}(y^{\prime})-P_{t}(y/{\sqrt{2}},y^{\prime})\rvert\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Lambda_{n}^{2}C(\varphi)\int_{t=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(t\vee 1)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}t\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0,

(6.104)

where the last inequality holds because
1-{\mathrm{e}}^{-a}\leq a
for all
a\geq 0
, and
\lvert\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{2}-\lvert y/{\sqrt{2}}-y^{\prime}\rvert^{2}\rvert\lesssim\lvert y\rvert^{2}+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{2}
. To show that the other limit vanishes, we use the following two observations:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{y\in{\rm supp}(\phi)}\Lambda_{n}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\int_{y^{\prime}}\lvert P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})-P_{t}({\varepsilon}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\rvert\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t=0,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\sup_{y^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{s=0}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{s}\varphi_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t<\infty.

Here, the equality in the first line is justified in the same way as in the proof of (
6.104
), and the inequality in the second line holds for all sufficiently large
q
as a direct consequence of
[
18
, Lemma 5.4]
. For any such
q
, we perform the following calculation to justify the limit:

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{3}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{s}(0,{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t-s}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{3}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}P_{s}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\varphi(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t-s}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{3}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}\lvert P_{s}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})-P_{s}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\rvert\varphi(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t-s}\varphi_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t-s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Lambda_{n}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\lvert P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})-P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\rvert\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad=\Lambda_{n}\int_{y}\int_{y^{\prime}}\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\lvert P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})-P_{t}({{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}},{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime})\rvert\varphi(y^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}t\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl(\int_{t=0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{s=0}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y^{\prime}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{s}\varphi_{n}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{s})\right]{\mathrm{d}}s{\mathrm{d}}t\biggr){\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime}\phi(y){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0,

(6.105)

where the second inequality and the last equality both use elementary properties of Laplace transforms, and the limit can be justified by using the two observations mentioned above.

In summary, (
6.102
) follows from (
6.104
) and (
6.105
) via (
6.103
).

Step 2-2.
The second limit states that for all large enough
q>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t){\mathrm{d}}t.

(6.106)

To see (
6.106
), we use (
6.102
), thereby obtaining

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}\Lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{y}\phi(y){\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}y/{\sqrt{2}}}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{{\varepsilon}_{n}}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right]{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{4\pi}{\log(q/\beta)}=\int_{t=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(t){\mathrm{d}}t,

where the second equality follows from
[
18
, (5.12)]
(see
[
23
, Section 4.4]
for an alternative proof of this equality), and the last equality uses
[
18
, (5.2)]
.

Step 2-3.
We now proceed to prove (
6.100
). By using Lévy’s continuity argument (see
[
18
, Lemma 4.18, p.165]
), (
6.106
) yields

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{\tau}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{\tau})\right]{\mathrm{d}}\tau=\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau,\quad\forall\;t\geq 0.

Hence, by the bounded continuity of
\tau\mapsto P_{t-\tau}F_{x,T_{0}}(0)
,
0\leq\tau\leq t
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\tau=0}^{t}{\mathfrak{s}}^{\beta}(\tau)P_{t-\tau}F_{x,T_{0}}(0){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{\tau}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{\tau})\right]P_{t-\tau}F_{x,T_{0}}(0){\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\Lambda_{n}{\mathbb{E}}^{B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}}_{0}\left[\exp\left(\Lambda_{n}\int_{r=0}^{t}\varphi_{{\varepsilon}_{n}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{r}){\mathrm{d}}r\right)F_{x,T_{0}}(B^{\mathbf{i}_{0}}_{t})\right],

where the last equality holds since
{\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{0}^{t}h(s){\mathrm{d}}s}=1+\int_{0}^{t}h(s){\mathrm{e}}^{\int_{s}^{t}h(r){\mathrm{d}}r}{\mathrm{d}}s
. This proves (
6.100
).

\blacksquare

Proof of (
2.25
) in Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
).

For any nonnegative
g_{1},g_{2}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}_{pd}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, it holds almost surely that

\displaystyle\int_{s_{1}=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{1}}\int_{s_{2}=s_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}g_{j}(x_{j},x_{j}){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{2},{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{1},{\mathrm{d}}s_{1})

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s_{1}=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{1}}\int_{x_{1}^{\prime}}\int_{s_{2}=s_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{2}}\int_{x_{2}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}g_{j}(x_{j}^{\prime},x_{j}){\langle}M_{n},M_{n}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{2}^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x_{2},{\mathrm{d}}s_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\langle}M_{n},M_{n}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{1}^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x_{1},{\mathrm{d}}s_{1})

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s_{1}=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{1}}\int_{\widetilde{x}_{1}}\int_{s_{2}=s_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{x}_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}g_{j}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}_{j}+x_{j},x_{j})\Lambda_{n}X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}_{j}+x_{j},s_{j})X_{n}(x_{j},s_{j})\phi(\widetilde{x}_{j})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}_{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1},

where the first equality follows from Corollary
3.8
and (
4.87
), and the last equality applies (
3.3b
). Hence, applying Theorem
3.4
(1
∘
) via Fatou’s lemma yields that, for any
p\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\int_{s_{1}=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{1}}\int_{s_{2}=s_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}g_{j}(x_{j},x_{j}){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{2},{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}){\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x_{1},{\mathrm{d}}s_{1})\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{s_{1}=T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{1}}\int_{\widetilde{x}_{1}}\int_{s_{2}=s_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{x_{2}}\int_{\widetilde{x}_{2}}\biggl(\prod_{j=1}^{2}g_{j}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}_{j}+x_{j},x_{j})\phi(\widetilde{x}_{j})\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Lambda_{n}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}_{1}+x_{1},s_{1})X_{n}(x_{1},s_{1})X_{n}({\varepsilon}_{n}\widetilde{x}_{2}+x_{2},s_{2})X_{n}(x_{2},s_{2})]{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}_{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{x}_{1}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}{\mathrm{d}}s_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\lambda,\phi,\lVert X_{0}\rVert_{\infty},T,p)\biggl(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\int_{x_{j}}g_{j}(x_{j},x_{j}){\mathrm{d}}x_{j}\biggr)(T_{2}-T_{1})^{2-p}.

Since
g_{1},g_{2}
are arbitrary, the last inequality is enough to prove (
2.25
).

\blacksquare

Proof of (
2.26
) in Theorem
2.7
(2
∘
).

The proof proceeds similarly to that of (
2.25
), except we now use Theorem
3.4
(2
∘
) in place of (1
∘
), together with the following almost sure convergence:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{x}\int_{x^{\prime}}f^{1/2}(x^{\prime})f^{1/2}(x)\begin{bmatrix}\int_{z_{2}}P_{a_{2}}(y_{2},z_{2})X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},s)\\
\int_{z_{1}}P_{a_{1}}(y_{1},z_{1})X_{n}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\langle}M_{n},M_{n}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime},{\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{s=0}^{t}\int_{x}f(x)\begin{bmatrix}\int_{z_{2}}P_{a_{2}}(y_{2},z_{2})X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{2},s)\\
\int_{z_{1}}P_{a_{1}}(y_{1},z_{1})X_{\infty}({\mathrm{d}}z_{1},s)\end{bmatrix}_{\times}{\langle}M_{\infty},M_{\infty}{\rangle}({\mathrm{d}}x,{\mathrm{d}}s).

More precisely, besides (
3.37
), this convergence depends on the uniform convergence of the integrands as functions of
s\in[0,T]
as
n\to\infty
(see Corollary
3.8
).

\blacksquare

7
Heat kernel expansions and integral bounds

In this section, we collect two sets of properties of the two-dimensional heat kernels

P_{t}(x,y)=P_{t}(x-y)
defined in (
1.10
). The properties of the first set are gathered in Lemma
7.1
below. It shows expansions that refine or restate some of the results established in
[
17
,
18
]
.

Lemma 7.1.

(1
∘
)
For all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
and
T\in(0,\infty)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}P_{2r}(y){\mathrm{d}}r&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\log\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)-\frac{\gamma_{\sf EM}}{4\pi}+{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(7.1)

Here,
{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}(a)
is a nonnegative function defined by

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}(a)

\displaystyle\;{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}\,\int_{0}^{\tfrac{1}{a}}\frac{(\mathds{1}_{(0,1]}(t)-{\mathrm{e}}^{-t})}{4\pi t}{\mathrm{d}}t+\frac{\log^{-}a}{4\pi},\quad a\in(0,\infty),

where
\log^{-}x\,{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}}-\log(x\wedge 1)
is the negative part of
\log x
.
In particular,

\displaystyle 0\leq{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}(a)\lesssim a^{-1}\mathds{1}(a\geq 1)+(1+\log^{-}a)\mathds{1}(a<1).

(7.2)

(2
∘
)
For all
{\varepsilon},\delta\in(0,\infty)
with

{\varepsilon}/\delta^{1/2}\leq 1

and all
M\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\lvert x\rvert\leq M}\lvert P_{t}({\varepsilon}x+y)-P_{t}(y)\rvert\leq C(M)({\varepsilon}/\delta^{1/2})P_{2t}(y),\;\forall\;y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;t\geq\delta.\end{split}

(7.3)

Also, for all
\delta\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lvert P_{t_{1}}(x)-P_{t_{0}}(x)\rvert&\leq C(\delta)\lvert t_{1}-t_{0}\rvert,\quad\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;t_{1},t_{0}\geq\delta,\\
\lvert P_{t_{1}}(x)-P_{t_{0}}(x)\rvert&\leq\lvert t_{1}-t_{0}\rvert{\mathrm{e}}^{-\lvert x\rvert^{2}/\delta}\left(\frac{\lvert x\rvert^{2}+2\max\{t_{0},t_{1}\}}{4\pi\delta^{3}}\right),\quad\forall\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;t_{1},t_{0}\geq\delta.\end{split}

(7.4)

Remark 7.2.

The following asymptotic expansion as
{\varepsilon}\to 0
, a consequence of an integral representation of the Macdonald function
K_{0}

[
51
, (5.10.25), p.119]

and a series expansion of
K_{0}

[
51
, (5.7.11), p.110]
, can also be deduced from (
7.1
): for all
q\in(0,\infty)
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}}{4\pi t}\exp\left(-\frac{{\varepsilon}^{2}\lvert x\rvert^{2}}{4t}\right){\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{\log{\varepsilon}^{-1}}{2\pi}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\frac{\log 4-\log q}{2}-\log\lvert x\rvert-\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}\right)+o(1).

(7.5)

More specifically, to obtain (
7.5
) from (
7.1
), note
\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-qt}f(t){\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}q{\mathrm{e}}^{-qT}\int_{0}^{T}f(t){\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}T
and the following representation of
\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}
:
\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{e}}^{-T^{\prime}}\log T^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}T^{\prime}

(cf.
[
51
, (1.3.19), p.8]
).
\blacklozenge

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

(1
∘
) The expansion in (
7.1
) follows from a slight modification of the proof of

[
17
, Theorem 4.18, pp.2946–2947]
. By the change of variables
v=r/\lvert y\rvert^{2}
, we can write

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P_{2r}(y){\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}}\frac{({\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{1}{4v}}-\mathds{1}_{[1,\infty)}(4v))}{4\pi v}{\mathrm{d}}v+\mathds{1}_{[1,\infty)}\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{4\pi}\log\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi}\log\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(\mathds{1}_{[1,\infty)}(4v)-{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{1}{4v}})}{4\pi v}{\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}})/4}^{\infty}\frac{(\mathds{1}_{[1,\infty)}(4v)-{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{1}{4v}})}{4\pi v}{\mathrm{d}}v-\mathds{1}_{(0,1)}\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{4\pi}\log\left(\frac{4T}{\lvert y\rvert^{2}}\right).

(7.6)

Note that the second and third terms on the right-hand side of (
7.6
) are equal to the next integral with
a=0
and with
a=4T/\lvert y\rvert^{2}
, respectively:

\displaystyle\int_{a/4}^{\infty}\frac{(\mathds{1}_{[1,\infty)}(4v)-{\mathrm{e}}^{-\frac{1}{4v}})}{4\pi v}{\mathrm{d}}v=\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{a}}\frac{(\mathds{1}_{(0,1]}(t)-{\mathrm{e}}^{-t})}{4\pi t}{\mathrm{d}}t,

(7.7)

where the equality follows by using the change of variables
t=1/(4v)
. In particular,
when
a=0
, the right-hand side of (
7.7
) equals
\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}/(4\pi)
by a standard integral representation of
\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}

[
51
, the display next to (1.3.19), p.8]
. Hence, (
7.6
) is enough to get (
7.1
).

It remains to show (
7.2
). To get the nonnegativity of
{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}(a)
, it is enough to
note that the integral on the right-hand side of (
7.7
) is positive when
a\geq 1
and decreases to
\gamma_{\mathsf{E}\mathsf{M}}/(4\pi)>0
over
0<a<1
as
a\searrow 0
.
Also, the second inequality in (
7.2
) can be seen by applying the bound
1-{\mathrm{e}}^{-t}\leq t
, valid for all
t\geq 0
, to the integral in
{\varepsilon}_{\ref{limT:glue}}(a)
. We have proved all of the properties stated in (1
∘
).

(2
∘
) The estimates in (
7.3
) and (
7.4
) restate
[
18
, Lemma 4.16 (2
∘
) and (3
∘
), pp.161–162]
.

\blacksquare

The next lemma gives the second set of properties. It shows bounds for various integrals used in the earlier sections; the simple bound in (
7.8
) is the central tool.

Lemma 7.3.

For all
T_{0},T_{1}\in(0,\infty)
,
a\in(0,\infty)
,

p,p^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
,
p_{1},p_{2}\in(0,\infty)
and
y,y^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\frac{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}},

(7.8)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t=0}^{T_{0}}\frac{(\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}}+t^{p_{2}})P_{at}(0)P_{t}(x^{\prime})}{1+\lvert y-x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(a,p,p_{1},p_{2})\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{2}(T_{0}^{jp_{j}/2}+T_{0}^{(jp_{j}+p)/2})}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}},

(7.9)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p^{\prime}}P_{T_{0}}(x^{\prime})}{1+\lvert y-x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,p^{\prime})\frac{T_{0}^{p^{\prime}/2}+T_{0}^{(p^{\prime}+p)/2}}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}},

(7.10)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{P_{T_{0}}(y,x^{\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\frac{1+T_{1}^{p/2}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}P_{T_{0}+2T_{1}}(y-y^{\prime}).

(7.11)

Here, we adopt the continuity convention that
\lvert y^{\prime\prime}\rvert^{0}=1
when
0=y^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Proof.
We prove (
7.8
)–(
7.11
) in Steps 1–4 below, respectively.

Step 1.
The required inequality in (
7.8
) follows upon noting that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}\frac{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}{1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}\leq\frac{C(p)}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}\frac{1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{1+\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}\leq C(p)\frac{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}},

where the first equality uses the bound

\lvert y\rvert^{p}\leq(\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert)^{p}\leq C(p)(\lvert y-y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p})
.

Step 2.
By (
7.8
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\int_{t=0}^{T_{0}}\frac{(\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}}+t^{p_{2}})P_{at}(0)P_{t}(x^{\prime})}{1+\lvert y-x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(p)}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}\int_{x^{\prime}}(\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}}+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}+p})\int_{t=0}^{T_{0}}\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}at^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{2t}\right){\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{C(p)}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}\int_{t=0}^{T_{0}}t^{-1+p_{2}}\int_{x^{\prime}}(1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p})\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}at}\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{2t}\right){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C(a,p)}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}\left[\int_{x^{\prime}}\left(\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}-2}+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p_{1}+p-2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{2T_{0}}\right){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}+\int_{t=0}^{T_{0}}(t^{-1+p_{2}}+t^{-1+p_{2}+p/2}){\mathrm{d}}t\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(a,p,p_{1},p_{2})\frac{T_{0}^{p_{1}/2}+T_{0}^{(p_{1}+p)/2}+T_{0}^{p_{2}}+T_{0}^{p_{2}+p/2}}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}},

(7.12)

where the second inequality applies the formula
\int t^{-2}{\mathrm{e}}^{-b/t}{\mathrm{d}}t={\mathrm{e}}^{-b/t}/b+C
, for
b\neq 0
and
t>0
, and the following scaling property:

\displaystyle\forall\;\widetilde{p}\in(-2,\infty),\;t\in(0,\infty),\quad\int_{x^{\prime}}\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{\widetilde{p}}\exp\left(-\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{2}}{2t}\right){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}=C(\widetilde{p})t^{(\widetilde{p}+2)/2},

(7.13)

and the inequality in (
7.12
) is also obtained by using (
7.13
). We obtain (
7.9
) from (
7.12
).

Step 3.

The proof of (
7.10
) is similar. Applying (
7.8
) and (
7.13
) in the same order, we get

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p^{\prime}}P_{T_{0}}(x^{\prime})}{1+\lvert y-x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{(\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p^{\prime}}+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p^{\prime}+p})P_{T_{0}}(x^{\prime})}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}\leq C(p,p^{\prime})\frac{T_{0}^{p^{\prime}/2}+T_{0}^{(p^{\prime}+p)/2}}{1+\lvert y\rvert^{p}}.

Step 4.

To prove (
7.11
), we first change variables to get the following equality:

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{P_{T_{0}}(y,x^{\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\frac{P_{T_{0}}(y-y^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime\prime})}{1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}\frac{P_{T_{0}}(y-y^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime\prime})(1+\lvert x^{\prime\prime}\rvert^{p})}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime},

where the last inequality uses (
7.8
). To continue, we rewrite the right-hand side so that

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}\frac{P_{T_{0}}(y,x^{\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{1+\lvert x^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(p)}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}P_{T_{0}}(y-y^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C(p)T_{1}^{p/2}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}P_{T_{0}}(y-y^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})P_{T_{1}}(x^{\prime\prime})\left|\frac{x^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{2T_{1}}}\right|^{p}{\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(p)}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}P_{T_{0}+T_{1}}(y,y^{\prime})+\frac{C(p)T_{1}^{p/2}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}\int_{x^{\prime\prime}}P_{T_{0}}(y-y^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})P_{2T_{1}}(x^{\prime\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\frac{1+T_{1}^{p/2}}{1+\lvert y^{\prime}\rvert^{p}}P_{T_{0}+2T_{1}}(y-y^{\prime}).

Here, we have applied the Chapman–Kolmogorov equation to derive the two terms on the right-hand side of the second inequality; the derivation of the second term also uses the bound
{\mathrm{e}}^{-a}a^{p}\leq C(p){\mathrm{e}}^{-a/2}
for all
a\geq 0
. The last inequality proves (
7.11
). The proof of Lemma
7.3
is complete.

\blacksquare
